- **O CI parou de rodar o mesmo trabalho duas vezes.** `Integration` e `Security SAST` disparavam por `push` e por `pull_request` no mesmo commit, e a chave de concorrência (`github.ref`) os colocava em grupos distintos, então ambos rodavam. A chave passou a ser a branch, então o run do PR cancela o do push — metade dos runs, e sobra o que testa o merge commit (#129).

## [0.45.0] - 2026-07-24

### Added
- **Roadmap M16–M20 — o backlog de issues abertas virou plano rastreável.** Cinco milestones agrupando as 13 issues que exigem trabalho, por coesão técnica: identidade fail-closed, CI que prova o que diz provar, qualidade do sinal de entidades, dívida do caminho de recall, e higiene de dependências (#121, #84, #102, #104, #129, #116, #125, #127, #124, #123, #118).


### Fixed
- **Segurança: no modo multi-tenant, um token OIDC sem tenant era servido no workspace padrão.** O caminho OIDC do servidor MCP não resolvia workspace e caía no `default`, o que num deployment multi-tenant serviria dados de outro tenant. Agora, com o mesmo sinal de fail-closed que a API REST já usa, um token que não resolve tenant é recusado em vez de servido — deployments single-tenant seguem funcionando por padrão (#84). O sinal de fail-closed (`THEMEMORY_REQUIRE_CREDENTIAL`) agora é lido também pelo servidor MCP, não só pela API REST — antes a guarda existia mas nenhum caminho de produção a ativava.
- **Segurança: uma chave de workspace usada direto ainda permitia impersonação; agora exige sinal de gateway.** O `/review` mostrou que tratar "chave sem usuário" como "veio do gateway" era falso — um operador pode emitir uma chave de workspace e usá-la direto. O cabeçalho `X-Theo-User-Id` agora só é honrado quando a credencial carrega o escopo `memory:platform-admin` (que só a credencial do gateway tem); e uma chave ligada a um usuário com dado corrompido é recusada, em vez de silenciosamente rebaixada para confiar no cabeçalho (#121).
- **Segurança: uma chave de API podia operar como outro usuário do mesmo workspace.** Com o servidor MCP exposto direto (sem gateway), o cabeçalho `X-Theo-User-Id` — controlado por quem chama — sobrepunha a identidade da credencial, permitindo ler, escrever e apagar no escopo alheio. A identidade agora vem sempre da credencial autenticada; o cabeçalho só é honrado quando a credencial é de workspace e não carrega usuário, que é o caso legítimo do gateway (#121).

## [0.44.0] - 2026-07-24

### Added
- **M15 /review — o impulso por entidades ficou observável.** Passa a existir sinal de que o impulso está vivo a cada busca, para que ele não possa voltar a morrer em silêncio como aconteceu da v0.1 ao M15 (M15) (#122).
- **M15 — o impulso pode reduzir resultados quando o corte de relevância é alto.** Com o impulso ligado, memórias sem vínculo de entidade perdem cerca de um terço do score normalizado. No corte padrão isso não muda nada; acima de ~0.67 elas passam a ficar de fora. Medido: 75 exclusões em 25 buscas com corte 0.8. É comportamento defensável — um resultado sem vínculo está mesmo mais longe do máximo alcançável quando há evidência de entidade — mas ninguém adivinharia isso pelas notas de release, então está aqui e rastreado em #127 (M15) (#122).
- **M15 T4.1 — benchmark medido do ganho.** Contra Postgres real, com corpus de 100 memórias: a precisão do primeiro resultado sobe de **0.64–0.68 para 1.00** quando o impulso por entidades está ligado, sem nenhuma memória sair do conjunto recuperado, ao custo de **+6 ms por busca**. A faixa na linha de base é honesta: sem o impulso os candidatos empatam e a ordem entre empatados varia entre execuções — com o impulso ligado o resultado é estável. Método, ressalvas e comando de reprodução em `docs/benchmarks/entity-boost-m15.md` (M15) (#122).
- **M15 T3.2/T3.3 — evidência de que o ganho é real e não custa nada.** Contra Postgres real: duas memórias com o mesmo texto e o mesmo embedding, uma ligada à entidade da busca — a ligada vem primeiro. E as duas guardas: uma memória irrelevante ligada à entidade **não** é ressuscitada, e nenhuma memória some do resultado por efeito do impulso (M15) (#122).
- **M15 T3.1 — o terceiro sinal do recall deixa de ser inerte.** Uma memória ligada às entidades citadas na busca passa a ranquear acima de uma memória equivalente sem esse vínculo. Desde a v0.1 o componente existia na fórmula e valia sempre zero: o que a documentação chamava de "semântico + BM25 + entidades" era, na prática, "semântico + BM25". Instalações sem grafo populado continuam com o comportamento anterior (M15) (#122).
- **M15 T2.1/T2.2 — leitura dos vínculos de entidade dos candidatos.** O sistema passa a saber, em uma única consulta, quais entidades estão ligadas às memórias que uma busca recuperou — a fonte do sinal estrutural que faltava ao ranking (M15) (#122).
- **M15 T1.2 — contagem de entidades por memória.** Cada memória recuperada agora sabe quantas das entidades citadas na busca estão ligadas a ela, que é o número que alimenta o peso do sinal estrutural no ranking (M15) (#122).
- **M15 T1.1 — casamento entre a query e as entidades já persistidas.** Uma query que menciona "São Paulo" ou o campo `user_id` agora identifica as entidades correspondentes no acervo, preservando acentuação e nomes com mais de uma palavra. É a peça que faltava para o terceiro sinal do recall deixar de ser inerte (M15) (#122).


### Changed
- **A opção `entityBoost` volta a significar o que promete.** Ela desligava a extração de entidades na escrita, mas na leitura não governava nada — porque o impulso por entidades era somado como zero constante desde a v0.1. Agora vale dos dois lados: desligar a opção devolve exatamente o comportamento anterior (M15) (#122).


### Fixed
- **Entidade aposentada influenciava o ranking.** Quando uma entidade é substituída por outra (M13), a linha antiga continuava podendo receber novas ligações e, com o impulso ligado, empurrava resultados — enquanto a listagem, o painel e o grafo já a escondiam. Um impulso vindo de algo que nenhuma tela mostra (M15) (#122).
- **A busca de vínculos respeitava apenas o workspace.** Agora aplica o escopo completo — usuário, sessão e agente — o mesmo predicado usado no resto do sistema, em vez de herdar a garantia do conjunto de candidatos (M15) (#122).
- **Uma consulta de entidades pendurada travava a busca inteira.** Só erro lançado degradava; agora há teto de 750 ms e a busca segue sem o terceiro sinal em vez de bloquear quem chamou (M15) (#122).
- **`get_memory` retornava memória JÁ ESQUECIDA (achado do live test M14).** O `forget` faz soft-delete (`t_invalid`) e todos os outros read paths filtram, mas a leitura por id (`Memory.get` → `vectorStore.get`) não filtrava — o dono dava `forget` e o `get_memory` seguia devolvendo o conteúdo. Corrigido no read PÚBLICO (`LocalMemory.get` retorna `null` quando `t_invalid` está setado); o `get` cru do store continua devolvendo a linha de propósito, para o `forget({hard:true})` poder fazer a erasure GDPR de uma memória já esquecida. Regressão coberta em `isolation.test.ts`.

## [0.43.0] - 2026-07-24

### Added
- **M14 Phase 5 — gate de isolamento (workspace, user) + benchmark do recall por-prompt.** Teste HARD GATE `tests/integration/kit-recall-parity/isolation.test.ts` (contra `ankane/pgvector:v0.5.1`): prova cross-TENANT E cross-USER para as 4 superfícies novas (get_memory, list_entities, update_memory, delete_entities) + o cenário fail-closed #252 (delete sem user confirmado aborta, nada removido) — 5/5. Benchmark `recall-coverage-benchmark.test.ts` (determinístico, sem API key): a fração `last_accessed_at IS NULL` cai de **100% baseline → 80%** (query constante do nome do projeto, comportamento do hook antigo — reproduz o ~79% medido em produção) → **5%** (12 queries distintas por-prompt, hook novo) = **75 pontos** de redução. Números persistidos em `knowledge-base/implementations/claude-code-kit-recall-parity-benchmark.json`. (M14)
- **M14 T4.2 — manifesto de distribuição (marketplace).** `.claude-plugin/plugin.json` + `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json` (formato do kit de referência): declaram os 8 hooks (4 novos M14 + 4 existentes) + o comando `/onboard` + `userConfig` (URL + token). README documenta os hooks novos + a seção `/onboard`. Empacota o kit para instalação via marketplace. (M14)
- **M14 T4.1 — wizard `/onboard` idempotente.** Novo `theo-onboard.mjs`: detecta `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` no cwd e os importa como memórias de perfil (reusa o conceito do `themory import claude-code` do M12, adaptado ao `post()` do kit). Idempotência por hash de conteúdo (marker local por-projeto) — re-rodar com conteúdo igual não duplica. Scope `(workspace,user)` do Principal server-side (ADR-M14-1): o body só carrega conteúdo, nunca identidade; sem token não importa (EC-2). (M14)
- **M14 T3.2 — guarda anti-escrita em MEMORY.md.** Novo hook `theo-block-memory-write.mjs` (PreToolUse `Write|Edit`): bloqueia (exit 2) escrita no arquivo `MEMORY.md` exato (basename — `MEMORY.md.bak` passa) e redireciona ao `remember`/MCP, evitando memória órfã invisível ao recall (blueprint T5). Qualquer falha própria → allow (exit 0): a guarda nunca trava uma escrita legítima. (M14)
- **M14 T3.1 — tool-context: recall do erro no PostToolUse Bash.** Novo hook `theo-tool-context.mjs`: quando um comando Bash falha (Traceback / panic / `error[Exxx]` / "Error" repetido), extrai a linha de erro mais acionável (a ponta do traceback) e busca `/v1/recall` com ela — traz a última vez que vimos esse erro e como resolvemos, injetado via `PostToolUse.additionalContext`. Pula `git commit/merge/rebase/push` (não é bug reproduzível) e output < 50 chars (ruído). Scope do Principal server-side (ADR-M14-1); sem token não busca (EC-2); qualquer falha → emite nada, exit 0 (nunca bloqueia o turno). Registrado no `PostToolUse` matcher `Bash`. (M14)
- **M14 T2.4 — delete_entities MCP tool + forgetEntity core primitive (fail-closed #252).** Novo primitivo `MemoryEntityStore.forgetEntity({ name, workspaceId, filters })`: remove a entidade (nó), seus links `memory_entities` e suas `entity_edges` em UMA transação, scoped a `(workspace, user)` — as MEMÓRIAS-fonte ficam intactas (só o grafo derivado, re-extraível, é removido). GUARDA fail-closed: se o filtro não carrega um user confirmado, lança `EntityForgetScopeError` ANTES de qualquer `pool.connect()` (a lição do cascade-delete theo-cloud#252 — escopo sub-restringido nunca vira wipe aberto). A tool MCP `delete_entities` monta o escopo do Principal via `getScope()`, nunca do argumento (ADR-M14-1); sem user → `MissingScopeError` na superfície, store nunca tocado. Idempotente (0/0/0 quando ausente). (M14)
- **M14 T2.3 — update_memory MCP tool (ADD-only via remember+forget).** Expõe "update" com o nome de paridade, mas implementado como remember(newText) ANTES de forget(oldId) — a ordem é a rede de segurança do edge EC-1: se o remember falha, o forget não executa e o dado antigo fica vivo (degrada para no-op, nunca perda). Preserva ADR-0003 (ADD-only) + history; retorna o id NOVO. (M14)
- **M14 T2.2 — list_entities MCP tool.** O agente lista as próprias entidades (que já escreve via M13) pela superfície MCP, reusando o read port do REST /entities. Scope (workspace,user) do Principal via getScope(), nunca do argumento (ADR-M14-1); schema strict rejeita args de identidade. memoryEntityStore injetado do pool no composition root. (M14)
- **M14 T1.2/T1.3 — autoria por origem de hook + registro do prompt-recall.** `theo-remember.mjs` agora marca `attributed_to: 'assistant'` (o resumo do Stop é do assistente, impede que vire "preferência do usuário"); refatorado para função pura testável. Prompt-recall registrado no `UserPromptSubmit` (theoAdditions idempotente). (M14)
- **M14 T1.1 — recall por-PROMPT no kit Claude Code (UserPromptSubmit).** Novo hook `theo-prompt-recall.mjs` busca pelo prompt do usuário (não pelo nome do projeto), scoped ao Principal `(workspace,user)` server-side; pula prompts < 20 chars; sem token → não busca (defesa da invariante). Fecha o gap medido (79% das memórias nunca lidas). (M14)
- Roadmap amended: added M14 Paridade de recall do kit Claude Code (leitura contínua + autoria) (`/roadmap-feature claude-code-kit-recall-parity`)

### Fixed
- **IDOR cross-user destrutivo em `update_memory` + `forget` (M14 /review BLOCKER, segurança).** O `/review` (7 agentes, 4 confirmaram) achou que o forget do `update_memory` e o tool `forget` usavam `workspaceScopedMemory(...).forget(id)` (workspace-only); `LocalMemory.forget` só checa workspace, sem user — então um user X podia soft-deletar a memória do user Y no MESMO workspace passando o id dela. Corrigido roteando ambos pelo forget SUBJECT-SCOPED (`routeScopedMemory(...).forget` → `ScopedMemory.forget` → `deleteInScope` sobre o filtro (workspace,user)): um id fora do escopo lança `MemoryNotFound`, nunca deleta. O gate de isolamento do `update_memory` era vacuoso (o `emptyJsonLlm` retornava `[]` → remember 0 items → o forget nunca executava) — agora usa um LLM que extrai fato, provando que o forget É exercido e o alvo sobrevive. Gate cross-user do `get_memory` endurecido para fail-closed (row com `user_id` != acting → escondido, incl. NULL). Provado por `isolation.test.ts` 5/5 vs pgvector real.
- **`forgetEntity`/`delete_entities` sem observabilidade (M14 /review, Princípio 3).** A única superfície destrutiva do grafo não emitia evento. Adicionado `MEMORY_EVENTS.entity_forgotten` (contagens removidas) via tracer OTel opcional (no-op sem exporter, wired em `run-mcp-server`); é o audit trail que substitui um tombstone soft-delete (a tabela `entities` não tem `t_invalid` — o grafo é projeção re-extraível; [ADR-0022](.claude/knowledge-base/adrs/ADR-0022-m14-entity-forget-hard-delete-of-reextractable-projection.md) amenda ADR-M14-2). `PgMemoryEntityStore` refatorado (helpers `runInTx`/`scopeIds` em `pg-tx.ts`).
- **M14 T1.2 — captura user-authored "remember this" wired de fato.** O `/review` achou que a atribuição user-side (Bullet 2) não tinha caller de produção (só um teste que injetava `'user'` na mão). Implementado `detectUserRememberDirective` no `theo-prompt-recall` (EN/PT): um diretivo explícito do usuário dispara um remember `attributedTo:'user'` fire-and-forget.
- **IDOR cross-tenant E cross-user em `Memory.get` / MCP `get_memory` (M14 Phase 5, segurança).** `LocalMemory.get(memoryId)` retornava a linha por `WHERE id=$1` SEM checar o workspace (diferente do `forget`, que já validava — EC-5/#82): um `get_memory` no workspace A lia memória do workspace B, e um user X lia a de Y no mesmo workspace. O gate de isolamento da Phase 5 pegou o vazamento. Corrigido em duas camadas: (1) core — `LocalMemory.get` agora retorna `null` quando o `workspace_id` da linha ≠ workspace ligado (espelha `forget` EC-5); (2) handler `get_memory` — passa a exigir user ativo e esconde linha de outro user no mesmo workspace (gate cross-user, DoD bullet 6). Provado por `isolation.test.ts` (cross-tenant + cross-user, 5/5 vs pgvector real) + 3 unit tests.
- **Abas SESSION/AGENT da tela Entities sempre vazias (#119).** `filtersFor()` no handler REST montava a Filters de tier único (`{ session_id }`) SEM o `user_id` — mas entidades de session/agent tier carregam AMBAS as colunas (o write path deriva o escopo da memória-fonte, M8/ADR-0021), e o predicado da listagem é `COALESCE(user_id,'') = COALESCE($2,'')`. Resultado: uma sessão com 237 memórias e 712 entidades linkadas renderizava "No entities". Agora o acting user (Principal ou header da edge) é threadado nos filtros dos três tiers — o mesmo que o caminho de fallback do handler já fazia. RED-GREEN em `entities-readport.test.ts`.


### Changed
- **CI: pipeline mais rápida (#116).** (1) `paths-ignore` (`.claude/**`, `**/*.md`) nos 3 workflows — pushes só-de-docs param de disparar build/deploy/testes/scan (o build de tag `v*` aponta para merge commits com código, nunca é filtrado). (2) Dockerfile: `COPY` reordenado (manifests → `pnpm install` → source) nos stages builder + production-deps, para o layer de install ser cache-hit quando só o código muda — evita recompilar os módulos nativos (isolated-vm/onnxruntime/sharp) a cada push. (3) Cache mount BuildKit no store pnpm (`--mount=type=cache`) em ambos os installs — deps inalteradas linkam do store persistido em vez de re-baixar.

## [0.42.0] - 2026-07-23

### Added
- **M13 noise retirement LIVE — DoD numérico fechado (#111).** `themory reextract --retire-mechanism --commit` na base app-dev retirou **1401** entidades de mecanismo não-reconfirmadas (1691 links + 761 edges), preservando as **819** âncoras de supersede e **0** órfãos. Queries canônicas pós: ALL-CAPS mecanismo **0** (≤10 ✅), ≤3 chars mecanismo **0** (≤10 ✅), mecanismo vivo total **0**, **7629/7629** entidades vivas semânticas. Apêndice T2.1 com SQL reproduzível em `knowledge-base/audits/m13-entity-noise-post-backfill-2026-07-23.md`.

- **M13 noise retirement — `themory reextract --retire-mechanism` (#111, follow-up do benchmark).** Aposenta o ruído mecânico (`PROPER|COMPOUND|QUOTED`) que o backfill completo não reconfirmou: remove links falsos + edges com endpoint no conjunto e coleta as linhas órfãs no contrato GC #110 — em UM statement CTE atômico por batch (BEGIN/COMMIT em pool alternaria conexões: transação ilusória, corrigida por construção). Âncoras de supersede JAMAIS coletadas (guarda no SELECT); dry-run é o DEFAULT e `--commit` é exigido explicitamente (lição theo-cloud#252); `--commit` sem `--retire-mechanism` → exit 2; evento `reextract_noise_retired` com contagens (Princípio 3). RED-GREEN: 1 unit (evento via tracer stub) + 3 integração pgvector (dry-run zero-write, commit preserva semânticas+âncoras e remove órfãs/edges, idempotência) + 1 E2E spawn (flags).
- **M13 T4.1 — backfill live + benchmark (#111).** Duas passadas do `themory reextract` na base app-dev (2 554 memórias processadas, 10 678 links novos, **819 entidades de mecanismo supersedidas**, ~2,6M tokens ≈ US$0,50, 52 min): **mecanismo novo na janela = 0** (write path D1 comprovado), memórias sem grafo 1029→218 (−79%), ruído mecânico visível 101→85 ALL-CAPS / 72→58 curtos. Metas ≤10 NÃO atingidas nesta fase — achado estrutural honesto: supersede por identidade re-tipa mas não aposenta ruído sem sucessor (LLM corretamente não re-emite HIGH/OK/PASS); fechamento via slice noise-retirement (precedentes #110/#115) antes do release. Relatório completo com amostras e reprodução em `knowledge-base/audits/m13-entity-noise-post-backfill-2026-07-23.md`.
- **M13 T3.2 — verbo CLI `themory reextract` (#111).** `themory reextract [--dry-run] [--resume] [--restart] [--limit N] [--concurrency N] [--json]` wired ao engine T3.1: dry-run exige SÓ `THEOMEM_PG_URI` (zero chamadas LLM por contrato — providers placeholder que lançam se tocados); run real exige `OPENAI_API_KEY` (mesmos env-specs compartilhados do server/MCP); `--restart` apaga o checkpoint. Exit codes: 0 limpo; 1 falhas parciais de LLM (checkpoint exclui os ids — `--resume` re-tenta); 2 config. Sentinela de locked names estendida com `reextract` (extensão sancionada M13, mesmo processo M12; CLAUDE.md § Locked names emendado). RED-GREEN: 3 E2E spawn contra dist + sentinela.
- **M13 T3.1 — engine de re-extração de entidades (#111, ADR D3).** `packages/cli/src/reextract/` (composition root — o domínio não conhece o job): seleção SQL (memórias vivas não-skill sem grafo OU com entidade de mecanismo viva), chunks por contagem+bytes (item único acima do teto processa sozinho, nunca skip silencioso), semáforo bounded (default 5), re-extração via primitivas públicas (prompt Phase 2 → `AdditiveExtractionSchema` → coerção `DEFAULT_ENTITY_TAXONOMY` → `linkEntities` com Filters derivados DA PRÓPRIA linha — isolação M8), supersede por identidade (`normalized_key` no MESMO escopo; identidade semântica pré-existente é reusada, nunca terceira linha), checkpoint resumível em arquivo (corrompido → erro tipado `ReextractCheckpointCorrupted` com instrução `--restart`), dry-run com estimativa de tokens e ZERO chamadas/escritas, input capado a 16K chars (precedente #109), evento `reextract_chunk_done` por chunk (Princípio 3). Exports públicos additive: `DEFAULT_ENTITY_TAXONOMY`, `linkEntities`, `normalizeEntityKey`, `applyOntologyToEntities`, `EntityLinkingDeps`/`LinkableItem` (slice ISP — linking só lê id+text). RED-GREEN: 8 unit (incl. semáforo com invariante de contador atômico) + 4 integração pgvector (E2E supersede idempotente, herança de escopo EC-1, identidade existente EC-3, dry-run zero-write).
- **M13 T2.1 — supersede auditável de entidades (#111).** Migration `0027`: `entities.superseded_by` (FK self-ref ON DELETE SET NULL) + índice parcial `entities_live_idx`. Novo `MemoryEntityStore.supersedeEntity({oldId,newId,workspaceId})`: UMA transação re-aponta links `memory_entities` (ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING para memória compartilhada) e endpoints de `entity_edges`, marca `old.superseded_by = new` — nunca DELETE (Princípio 3); alvo validado fail-closed ANTES de qualquer escrita; `superseded_by IS NULL` no UPDATE é o árbitro atômico (concorrência: exatamente 1 vencedor). RED-GREEN: 6 integração pgvector (incl. idempotência, alvo ausente, PK compartilhada, zero edges órfãs, corrida 2-callers).
- **M13 T2.2 — superfícies de leitura ignoram entidades supersedidas (#111).** `superseded_by IS NULL` em: listagem de entidades (+ summary LATERAL só com alvos vivos), expansão de grafo do recall (JOIN live — estado residual de mid-backfill nunca cria caminho), resolução de endpoint de edges (uma edge nova NUNCA ressuscita a linha supersedida — earliest-wins agora só entre vivas) e GC do hard-forget (âncoras de supersede — a linha com ponteiro E a referenciada por ele — jamais coletadas, edges seguem o mesmo conjunto). Pin default-path do Item 15 flipado para o contrato D1 (coerção default é observável). RED-GREEN: +3 integração.
- **M13 T1.1 — types semânticos por default no write path (#111).** O linking honra SEMPRE o type do LLM: sem ontology, coerção ao vocabulário fechado `DEFAULT_ENTITY_TAXONOMY` (PERSON|ORG|PLACE|PRODUCT|CONCEPT|OTHER — o menu que o prompt Phase 2 já pedia); out-of-vocab → OTHER + `ontology_type_coerced` observável; ontologias Item 15 seguem substituindo o vocabulário. Endpoints auto-criados e fallback compromise persistem OTHER — zero rótulo de mecanismo (PROPER/COMPOUND/QUOTED) em qualquer escrita nova. Pin obsoleto do infer:false (pré-Phase A) atualizado para o contrato D1. RED-GREEN: 3 unit + 2 integração pgvector.
- M13 cycle-plan COMPLETO: plano `llm-entity-reextraction` v1.1 SHIPPABLE 98/100 (zero caps; edge-case review com 3 MUST FIX absorvidos; deps-audit PASS — zero dependência nova, 0 HIGH/CRITICAL). Attested `8aabb4d4`. (#111)
- M13 cycle-discover COMPLETO: blueprint `llm-entity-reextraction` (SHIPPABLE_WITH_CAVEATS 89; 8/8 questões com citações path:line; achado central: o prompt já emite types semânticos e o linking os descarta no gate de ontology — entity-linking.ts:150) + ADRs D1 (destravar gate), D2 (supersede por identidade com `entities.superseded_by`), D3 (chunks+checkpoint+dry-run). (#111)
- M13 cycle-discover em andamento: plano de discovery `llm-entity-reextraction` v1.1 (SHIPPABLE_WITH_CAVEATS 89) + edge-case review + baseline de ruído de entidades persistido em `knowledge-base/audits/m13-entity-noise-baseline-2026-07-23.md` (1658 entidades, 129 ALL-CAPS, 106 ≤3 chars, 1029 memórias sem grafo — queries canônicas do DoD). Tooling: `select_next_milestone.py` aceita headers `##` e `###` (seleção do M13 falhava com 'no milestone headers found'). (#111)
- Roadmap amended: added M13 Re-extração LLM de entidades com types semânticos (`/roadmap-feature llm-entity-reextraction`) — corrige theo-memory#111 (Fase B do blueprint `multilingual-entity-extraction`)
- **Separação por sessão de ponta a ponta (modelo M8 realizado).** (a) `ScopedMemory.listSessions()` + `GET /v1/scopes/user/:id/sessions` — enumeração das sessões distintas de um `(workspace, user)` com contagem live + última atividade (nunca existia: as tabs SESSION do dashboard eram seletores de tipo sem seletor de instância); store duck-typed (`PgvectorStore`/`InMemoryVectorStore.listSessions`), erro tipado `SessionsListingNotSupported` → 501 honesto. (b) `POST /v1/reflect { sessionId }` agora threada a `SessionToGlobalConsolidationPolicy` (M9) — a promoção session→global era SDK-only, nenhum caminho REST a disparava. (c) Hooks do kit (`theo-remember`/`theo-recall`/`theo-precompact`) passam o `session_id` do Claude Code → escritas nascem na working memory da sessão `(workspace, user, session)` e recall lê SESSION ∪ GLOBAL; novo hook `theo-promote.mjs` (SessionEnd) dispara o reflect de promoção. RED-GREEN: unit (in-memory), integração pgvector isolada (2 casos), handler REST (3), wiring do reflect (2). Split de módulo: `memory-config.ts` extraído de `memory-store.ts` (budget 300 linhas).

- **Filtro por entidade na listagem de escopo (`?entity=`, theo-cloud#252).** `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/memories?entity=<nome>` retorna só as memórias LINKADAS à entidade (JOIN `memory_entities`→`entities.normalized_key`, workspace-bound; normalização server-side via `normalizeEntityKey`). É o filtro que faltava para o cascade-forget por entidade do dashboard — sem ele, o cascade deletava a primeira página GLOBAL (até 200 memórias arbitrárias). Novo `GetAllOptions.entity` + `PgvectorStore.listByEntity` (duck-typed, precedente skills); in-memory retorna página vazia honesta (não tem links); respostas filtradas omitem `total` (o `count()` é scope-wide) e ECOAM o filtro aplicado (`entity` no body) — contrato fail-closed: um provider antigo ignora query keys desconhecidas silenciosamente, e um cliente de cascade-delete DEVE abortar quando o eco não vem (incidente live 2026-07-23: 60+ memórias não relacionadas soft-deletadas pelo dashboard contra um provider sem o filtro; 100% restauradas via `t_invalid = NULL`). Split de módulo: `scoped-listing.ts` extraído de `scoped-memory.ts` (budget 300). RED-GREEN: integração pgvector isolada (3 casos: só linkadas + soft-deleted excluída, entidade inexistente vazia, cross-workspace não vaza) + handler (2).


### Changed
- Sentinela `MEMORY_EVENTS` 45→47: eventos `reextract_chunk_done` + `reextract_noise_retired` registrados na união tipada (extensão sancionada M13). (#111)

- **Graph entity extraction — global-grade redesign, Phase A (blueprint `multilingual-entity-extraction`, #111).** Deep research (prior art + web SOTA: arXiv:2501.13956, arXiv:2504.19413, GraphRAG, GLiNER) estabeleceu: (a) a extração LLM-primary multilingual já existe (Phase 2 emite `entities+relations` no idioma do input); (b) o upstream de referência NÃO roda extração de entidades em `infer=False` — a divergência de 2026-06-20 ligou o heurístico English-only no caminho verbatim e causou os 83% de ruído. **D1: `runInferFalsePath` não faz mais extração inline de entidades** (paridade upstream; RED-GREEN em `pipeline-helpers.test.ts`); o grafo de memórias verbatim/importadas virá do backfill LLM em batch (blueprint D2, próximo milestone). **D6: `CompromiseEntityExtractor` rebaixado a fallback EN-only endurecido** — strip de marcadores de scrub, QUOTED ≤6 palavras, gate de idioma via `eld` (texto não-EN pula os caminhos `nouns()`; capital solitário inicial descartado por POSIÇÃO, zero listas por idioma). Splits pelo budget de 300 linhas: `english-word-lists.ts`, `infer-false-path.ts`.

- CI: `concurrency` groups on `publish.yml` (group per ref; develop pushes supersede the in-flight run — a cancelled predecessor always has a successor deploy; tag runs never auto-cancelled) and `security-sast.yml` (mirrors `integration.yml`). Single self-hosted runner stops piling up redundant runs.


### Removed
- Suite `tests/unit/dogfood/` (10 arquivos, 20 testes) — pinava a maquinaria de journey-audit (run_journey_audit.py, drivers, catálogo, skill antiga) substituída pelo kit Cycle 6+1 (297e373); o veredito histórico do gate (`EVIDENCE_SUFFICIENT`, 2026-07-12) permanece registrado em CLAUDE.md/audits e no git. Surfaced pelo gate Step 5 do M13. (#111)
- Testes órfãos da app demo `examples/chat-with-memory` (a app foi removida em c3a1b63/2026-07-16; os testes ficaram referenciando módulos deletados — 8 falhas latentes que só apareciam na suite completa) + o resíduo `examples/` (só restava um package.json). Surfaced pelo gate Step 5 do M13. (#111)


### Fixed
- **M13 /review — 5 HIGH + fixes inline (#111).** (1) `supersedeEntity` repoint de edges agora DROPa a linha de origem colidente antes do UPDATE — uma UPDATE cega colidia com `entity_edges_scope_triple_uq` quando NEW já carrega a tripla idêntica, revertia a tx inteira e abortava o lote de reextract com livelock no resume (F-arch-1/F-dom-db-1); +2 testes (colisão + chaos mid-tx rollback). (2) `themory reextract --dry-run --retire-mechanism --commit` executava a deleção — flags contraditórias agora falham fechado, exit 2 (F-dom-cli-1, classe do incidente #252). (3) Tracer OTel agora é resolvido no composition root do CLI (`trace.getTracer`) — os eventos `reextract_chunk_done`/`reextract_noise_retired`/`ontology_type_coerced` eram no-op em produção (F-arch-7/F-dom-obs-1/F-wire-1). (4) `applyOntologyToEntities` normaliza variante de caixa ('person' → PERSON) antes de coagir — antes 'person' virava OTHER (F-tests-1/F-arch-4). (5) Guarda de âncora do retirement re-asserida DENTRO do DELETE via CTE `targets` — TOCTOU: um supersede concorrente podia deletar um pointee de auditoria (F-arch-2/F-dom-db-2).
- **M13 /review — MEDIUM.** `target_failed_parse` distinto de `target_failed_llm` (predicado de exit-1 soma todo `target_failed_*`); detecção de link silenciosamente falho (`target_failed_link`) ANTES do supersede — um `--retire-mechanism` posterior nunca deleta memória cujo grafo falhou; índice parcial `entities_superseded_by_idx` (migration 0028) para o guard NOT EXISTS + FK enforcement; membro morto `EntityLinkingDeps.ontology` removido; `MECHANISM_SQL` dedup para export único.


- Mock de `@usetheo/memory` em `run-mcp-server.test.ts` sem `PgRoutingAuditStore`/`SandboxedSkillExecutor` (espelho MCP do mesmo bug do run-server; 5 falhas latentes da suite completa). Suite completa agora 1287/1287 verde. (#111)
- **Gate Step 5 do M13 — suite completa vermelha em `run-server.test.ts` (pré-existente) + lint dos módulos reextract.** O mock de `@usetheo/memory` do teste não espelhava os imports `PgRoutingPolicyStore`/`PgRoutingAuditStore` que `run-server.ts` ganhou no M11/explainable-recall (vitest falhava o arquivo inteiro no load — só visível na suite completa); asserções desnecessárias + ordem de import nos módulos reextract corrigidas via `--fix`. (#111)

- **Agregação de entidades contava memórias soft-deletadas (#115).** `PgMemoryEntityStore.list` fazia o JOIN com `memories` sem `t_invalid IS NULL` — o dashboard oferecia "Forget N memories" para entidades cujos links já estavam esquecidos (o cascade então deletava 0, contagem enganosa). Agora só memórias vivas contam e uma entidade sem nenhum link vivo some da listagem. RED-GREEN em `tests/integration/entities/list.test.ts`. (#115)
- **MCP `add_skill`/`forget_skill`/`execute_skill` — repoId sem namespace de subject (#114).** Skills autoradas pelo fluxo canônico (agente → MCP) eram gravadas com `agent_id = <repoId>` bare, enquanto o dashboard/BFF lista por prefixo `{subject}:{repoId}` (convenção D3 do gateway) — toda skill criada via MCP ficava invisível na página Skills. Novo `namespacedRepoId`: com subject autenticado no scope → `{userId}:{repoId}` (paridade com `namespaceRepo` do BFF, incl. guarda de injetividade para `:` no subject); transporte sem auth (stdio local) → bare (backward-compatible). RED-GREEN: pins de `handlers.test.ts` flipados para `alice:acme` + caso no-auth bare. (#114)
- **Session tier sem vínculo de usuário via gateway (achado live da separação por sessão).** As keys mintadas pelo broker são workspace-admin (`Principal.userId` null) e o header `X-Theo-User-Id` só se aplicava a bodies sem tier — uma escrita session-scoped pela edge gravava `user_id NULL`, quebrando a enumeração de sessões E a união SESSION ∪ GLOBAL na leitura. Novo `actingUserId(c)` = userId do Principal (member, confinamento IDOR preservado — o header nunca alarga um member) ?? header da edge; threaded em TODOS os scope resolutions (remember/recall/reflect + scopes/sessions/episodes/metrics/entities/scoped-forget). Sem header e sem Principal → semântica admin inalterada. RED-GREEN em `session-user-binding.test.ts` (3 casos).

- **`GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/memories` — paginação por `offset` real (achado live do pager do dashboard).** O `offset` enviado pelo gateway nunca era parseado (o zod nem o conhecia) e o `getAll()` interno capava em 100 — toda página repetia os primeiros itens e nada além do item 100 era alcançável nem via cursor. Agora: `offset` no schema (coerce int ≥ 0, 422 em negativo), threading `GetAllOptions.offset` → `ScopedMemory.getAll` → `VectorStore.list(..., offset)` com `OFFSET` SQL sob a ordem determinística `created_at DESC, id ASC`; fast path (sem cursor/from/to) usa probe `limit+1` para `nextCursor` exato e devolve `total` real (`count()`); caminho legado fatia o conjunto filtrado com o offset. RED-GREEN: integração pgvector real + unit do handler. Surface-locks de `VectorStore.list` atualizados.

- **Reflect em escopo real (#112).** `executeReflection` construía UM prompt para o escopo inteiro; com 441 memórias a resposta truncava → `ReflectionLLMResponseError: response is not valid JSON` → 502 no dashboard (o followup EC-18 materializado). Agora o escopo é fatiado em batches (contagem `REFLECTION_BATCH_MAX_ITEMS=40` E chars `REFLECTION_BATCH_MAX_CHARS=48K` — a disciplina do #109), uma chamada LLM por batch, drafts concatenados; respostas cercadas por ```json``` são toleradas (fence-strip antes do parse). RED-GREEN em `execute.test.ts` (3 casos: split por contagem, split por bytes, fence).
- **GC de entidades no hard-forget (#110).** `PgvectorStore.deleteInScope(hard)` agora coleta, NA MESMA transação (via o `withTransaction` existente), as entidades que perderam seu ÚLTIMO link em `memory_entities` + edges tocando endpoint coletado — direcionado (O(k) sobre as entidades da memória deletada, nunca full-sweep). Novo evento `entity_gc_collected {entities_collected, edges_collected}` (Princípio 3; union 44 → 45). Integration RED-GREEN em `scoped-forget.test.ts` (entidade solo coletada, compartilhada sobrevive, edge órfã coletada).
- **Métricas do Overview com total real (#113).** `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/metrics` reportava `items.length` de um `getAll()` paginado (cap 100) — o dashboard mostrava "TOTAL MEMORIES = 100" num escopo de 440. Novo `ScopedMemory.count()` (COUNT real do store — o `list()` já o computava e descartava); handler usa `count()` para `total_memories`/`add_events`. O gateway repassa o endpoint, então o fix server-side corrige o dashboard ponta-a-ponta. Surface-lock `memory-store.test-d.ts` atualizado; unit RED-GREEN em `metrics-total.test.ts`.


- **`themory import claude-code` — byte-aware chunking (#109).** The live E2E of the M12 migrator on a real-size dataset (433 nodes) failed with `413 Request Entity Too Large`: `importBatch` chunked by COUNT only (`CHUNK_SIZE=500` → one request) while the REST server enforces a 1 MB bodyLimit. Chunks are now bounded by count AND serialized bytes (`CHUNK_MAX_BYTES` 512 KB — conservative half-limit for envelope/multi-byte headroom, `chunkByBytes`); a single item that alone exceeds the budget fails FAST with a typed `MigrationHttpError` before dialing (no silent skip). Regression tests: multi-chunk split under the count cap + oversized-single-item rejection; kit engine copy re-synced (byte-parity sentinel). (#109)
- **`themory import claude-code` — gateway capability-namespace path (#109 live E2E).** With `THEOMEM_MEMORY_PATH_PREFIX=/v1/memory`, the engine built `<base>/v1/memory/v1/remember/batch`; the Theo gateway REWRITES `/v1/memory/X → /v1/X`, so the request became `/v1/v1/remember/batch` → 404 (masked as 413 on big payloads — the body-limit fires before route match). `resolveImportEndpoint` now returns the prefix SEPARATELY and `importBatch` composes `prefix + /remember/batch` (theo-lib parity); no prefix keeps the native `/v1/remember/batch`. Unit-pinned both compositions.
- **`OpenAIEmbedder` — embedding input clamped to the model context (#109 live E2E).** Two real memory nodes (47K/25K chars) 400'd the embedder (`maximum context length is 8192 tokens`) and their whole remember failed with a sanitized INTERNAL_ERROR. `embed`/`embedBatch` now head-truncate the embedding input at `EMBED_INPUT_MAX_CHARS` (16000 chars ≈ 5-6K tokens, safe margin); the VERBATIM text is stored in full — only the vector sees the head (standard truncation practice).
- **`POST /v1/remember/batch` — batch items inherit the edge-injected user scope (single-remember parity, #109 live E2E).** `rememberHandler` applies the auth-trusted `X-Theo-User-Id` fallback to a tier-less body (`applyHeaderUserScope`), but the batch handler did not — a tier-less batch (the migrator through the gateway) 422'd `INVALID_SCOPE` wholesale. The fallback now applies PER ITEM before validation; without the header, tier-less items still fail closed (422). Unit: bind-to-header + fail-closed pinned.
- **`themory import claude-code` — scope semantics unified with the kit hooks (#109 live E2E).** The engine stamped `userId: <project-slug>` per item, so migrated rows landed in per-project user scopes while the kit hooks recall on the gateway's edge-injected `X-Theo-User-Id` — imports were INVISIBLE to recall. Items now carry NO `userId` by default (the edge binds the tier, theo-lib parity); `THEOMEM_MEMORY_USER_ID` (env → kit file) or `THEOMEM_MIGRATE_USER_ID` pin it explicitly (bare-REST runs). The project slug stays as provenance `metadata.project`, never the scope.
- **`themory import claude-code` — `CHUNK_SIZE` 500 → 75 (#109 live E2E).** Live measurement: a 183-item chunk took 275s server-side (~1.5s/item embedding), grazing Node fetch's non-extendable ~300s headers timeout ("fetch failed"). 75 items caps a request at ~2 min with margin; default pinned by unit test. `importBatch` tests split to `tests/unit/import-batch-http.test.ts` (300-line file budget).


### Security
- Override `sharp` ^0.33.5 → ^0.35.0 (0.35.3): fecha GHSA-f88m-g3jw-g9cj (libvips CVE-2026-33327/33328/35590/35591, HIGH) — o último bloqueio do gate Trivy do publish.
- Override transitivo `fast-uri` 3.1.2 → ^3.1.4 (via `@apidevtools/swagger-parser`→`ajv`): fecha CVE-2026-13676 + 2 HIGH que reprovavam o gate Trivy do publish (deploy-dev bloqueado).

## [0.41.0] - 2026-07-22

### Fixed
- M12 review fix-cycle (from the 2-agent `/review` of the M12 diff):
  - **F-arch-1 (BLOCKER) — the documented invocation was unreachable for kit users.** SKILL.md/README/RUNBOOK prescribed `npx --yes @usetheo/memory import claude-code`, but the package ships NO npm bin (Docker-only topology, ADR D2-a of `complete-workspace-publish-topology`) — the skill's primary path would fail on every kit machine. Fixed structurally: `install.sh` now SHIPS the migrator engine to `~/.claude/hooks/migrate-claude-code.mjs` (same channel as the hooks; 7 files copied) and the primary invocation everywhere is `node ~/.claude/hooks/migrate-claude-code.mjs` (checkout `dist/agent-tools/cli.js import claude-code` and Docker `-v ~/.claude:/root/.claude` as alternatives). A THIRD byte-parity sentinel (engine canonical ↔ kit copy) joins the scrub pair so the shipped copy can never diverge silently.
  - **F-arch-2/F-arch-5 — D2 resolution moved INSIDE the engine** (`parseKitEnvFile` + `resolveImportEndpoint` exported): the kit-shipped standalone path is zero-config (reads `~/.claude/theo-memory.env` itself, strips surrounding quotes — theo-lib parity), and `run-import.ts` becomes a pure flag→argv mapper that mutates no global state.
  - **SEC-05 — `metadata.type`/`file` now pass through the scrub** in `nodeToBatchItem` (body was scrubbed; frontmatter-derived fields were not).
  - **F-tests-1/F-arch-4 — dist-boot sentinel** (`tests/integration/import/cli-dist-import-boot.test.ts`): env-free `node dist/agent-tools/cli.js import claude-code --help` exits 0 + a dist dry-run against a temp `--source` reports without dialing — pins the `postbuild:copy-import` step.
  - **F-tests-3 — THE GOAL TEST now drives the shipped CLI layer**: both commits route through `main(['--commit','--source',…])` (endpoint via `THEOMEM_MIGRATE_URL`), not the engine internals.
  - Locked-names sentinel extended (+`import`, `claude-code`); installer test asserts the engine copy lands.

### Added
- M12 (T2.1+T3.1+T3.2): `/migrate-theo-memory` kit skill (orchestrates the CLI: dry-run → report → explicit confirmation → `--commit` → recall verification; NEVER re-implements the pipeline) + `install.sh` now copies kit `skills/` to `~/.claude/skills/`. THE GOAL TEST on real `ankane/pgvector`: a 3-node Claude-Code-shaped source imports with the secret-bearing node stored REDACTED (the secret never reaches the DB) and a re-run is fully idempotent (`deduped==3`, `imported==0`, row count unchanged). `themory` CLI locked names amended (+`import claude-code`); RUNBOOK + kit README document the flow.


- M12 (T1.1+T1.2): `themory import claude-code` — the Claude-Code-memory migrator is now a SHIPPED CLI subcommand (engine moved to `packages/cli/src/import/migrate-claude-code.mjs`; `scripts/migrate-claude-code-memory.mjs` stays as a back-compat re-export shim). Dry-run default, `--commit`, `--source`; endpoint/token resolved `THEOMEM_MIGRATE_*` → `THEOMEM_MEMORY_*` env → `~/.claude/theo-memory.env` (the kit file — zero-config on kit machines) → default. The scrub is now CANONICAL in the package (`packages/cli/src/import/theo-scrub.mjs`) with a byte-parity sentinel test failing CI on any divergence from the kit copy (M6-SEC-01). Build ships the `.mjs` engine into `dist/agent-tools/import/`. Unit: 24 green (env precedence 5, parity+behavior 2, migrator suite 17 via the shim).


- Roadmap amended: added M12 `themory import claude-code` — productize the Claude Code memory migration as a CLI subcommand + `/migrate-theo-memory` kit skill (`/roadmap-feature themory-import-cli`)

## [0.40.0] - 2026-07-22

### Fixed
- M11 review fix-cycle (from the 2-agent `/review` of the M11 diff):
  - **F-tests-1 (HIGH) — promotion decisions now audited.** The ROADMAP M11 DoD requires "every routing AND promotion decision" be a queryable audit record; only the routing (remember) path was wired. A session→global PROMOTION (M9 reflect) now emits a fail-soft audit row (`why: 'promotion-confirmation-threshold'`, `chosenScope: 'global'`) via `LocalMemoryConfig.routingAuditStore` threaded through `runReflection`; `wireMemoryFromPool` injects `PgRoutingAuditStore` so it fires in the deployed server/MCP/CLI. Integration (real pgvector): a durable-session-fact reflect promotes AND records a retrievable audit row.
  - **F-tests-2 (MEDIUM) — `POST /v1/remember/batch` now audits each item's routing decision** (was single-remember only).
  - **F-arch-2/F-arch-3 (LOW) — isolation hardening.** `routing-governance.ts` now resolves the Principal via `getPrincipal(c)` (absent → `DEFAULT_PRINCIPAL` owner) instead of `c.get('principal')`, so an absent Principal passes the SAME role/isolation branch — removes a special-case grant (unreachable in production behind bearerAuth, but consistent now).
  - **F-tests-4/F-tests-5 — coverage.** Added real-pgvector tests: the routing-policy routes roundtrip through the full REST stack (PUT→GET 200); and a THROWING audit store does not fail `POST /v1/remember` (wired fail-soft 201).
- **HONEST CAVEATS (M11, Rule 3):**
  - **The routing POLICY is a config + audit surface, NOT yet enforced on the write path** (F-tests-3). `RoutingPolicyStore` stores/validates/isolates the per-`(workspace, user)` policy (the DoD: config exists, fail-closed, admin-scoped, isolated), but no write path yet consults `defaultScope`/`llmRouting` to CHOOSE a scope — the agent's explicit M8 edge tier still decides placement. Enforcing the policy on writes is a follow-up.
  - **The audit write is `await`-ed on the `remember` hot path and always wired in the composition root** (F-arch-6) — one extra DB round-trip per write, and `routing_audit` grows one row per remember/promotion. Fail-soft covers rejections, not slow inserts; a retention sweep (reusing the recall-trace retention discipline) is a follow-up.

### Added
- M11 (routing governance, T4.1): the routing governance + isolation gate. STORE-level isolation is proven on real `ankane/pgvector` (`tests/integration/routing/governance-isolation.test.ts`): `PgRoutingPolicyStore` + `PgRoutingAuditStore` roundtrip a policy/audit for a `(workspace, user)` (absent → fail-closed default), and a `list`/`get` for one `(workspace, user)` never surfaces another's rows. The REST cross-user → 404 mapping is proven at the handler level (`tests/unit/routing-governance-handler.test.ts`); the two compose into the ROADMAP M11 isolation gate. Migrations `0025`/`0026` apply cleanly to the isolated DB.
- M11 (routing governance, T3.1): routing-governance REST surface. `GET/PUT /v1/routing-policy/:userId` (PUT is admin-scoped — a member → 403; invalid payload → 400) + `GET /v1/routing-audit/:userId`, mounted opt-in when the `routingPolicyStore`/`routingAuditStore` ports are wired (new `CreateServerOptions` fields, mirroring the `recallTraceStore` precedent). Workspace comes from the credential's Principal, the target user from the PATH; a MEMBER may only address its OWN `userId` — a cross-user path returns 404 (no existence leak, ADR D3), while an admin/owner manages any user in its workspace. The composition root (`themory server`) injects `PgRoutingPolicyStore` + `PgRoutingAuditStore` so the routes serve real data in production. Unit: `tests/unit/routing-governance-handler.test.ts` 4/4 (admin-gate 403, invalid 400, cross-user 404 on policy + audit).
- M11 (routing governance, T2.1): placement-decision audit (Princípio 3). A `RoutingAuditStore` DIP (`record`/`list`) captures every routing/promotion decision as a queryable record `{chosenScope, attribution(user|agent), decidedBy, why, at}`, reusing the shipped recall-trace governance shape (blueprint ADR D2 — no new engine, Rule 9). `list` is `(workspace, user)`-scoped (newest-first, capped) so it never returns another tenant's rows (ADR D3). The `recordRoutingDecision` fail-soft primitive never propagates a failed audit write — the decision already happened — emitting a new OTel event `routing_audit_write_failed` instead (`MEMORY_EVENTS` 43 → 44, Rule 8). Backed by the `routing_audit` table (migration `0026`, `(workspace_id, user_id, at)` index); `InMemoryRoutingAuditStore` + `PgRoutingAuditStore`. Unit: `routing-audit-store.test.ts` 4/4 (who/what/why, scoping, newest-first cap, fail-soft).
- M11 (routing governance, T1.1): per-`(workspace, user)` routing-policy config. A `RoutingPolicyStore` DIP (`get`/`put`) with a **fail-closed default** (`FAILCLOSED_DEFAULT_POLICY` — LLM routing OFF, `session` default scope, `confirmation_threshold` 3) so an absent/invalid config never disables isolation (blueprint ADR D1). Validated by the shipped `zod` (`RoutingPolicySchema`, strict) throwing a typed `InvalidRoutingPolicy` on a bad payload (Rule 9 — no new dep). Backed by the `routing_policies` table (migration `0025`) keyed by the M8 `(workspace_id, user_id)` isolation pair; `InMemoryRoutingPolicyStore` + `PgRoutingPolicyStore` impls. Unit: `routing-policy-store.test.ts` 4/4 (fail-closed default, put→get roundtrip, per-(workspace,user) isolation, zod rejection).

## [0.39.0] - 2026-07-22

### Fixed
- **M8 regression (BLOCKER, pre-merge CI) — user-tier path binding hijacked by the default owner Principal.** `principalUserId()` returned the Principal's userId unconditionally, so the single-tenant no-credential `DEFAULT_PRINCIPAL` (role `owner`, userId `DEFAULT_OWNER_USER_ID`) — set on every unauthenticated `createServer({ memory })` request — overrode the path/body user id on every user-tier route. A plain `DELETE /v1/scopes/user/alice/memories/:id` (and the matching list/metrics/entities/episodes/recall/reflect/remember paths) resolved to the owner's scope instead of `alice`, returning 404 on alice's own row and an empty list. Made `principalUserId()` **role-aware** (M8 ADR D6 "admin → path id"): an `admin`/`owner` Principal returns `undefined` so the path/body id is honored (workspace management + SDK-written-data compat), while a `member` returns its own userId (the F-arch-1 IDOR override stays closed). Fixes the 5 failing `scoped-forget` + `scope-usage-counter` integration tests that blocked PR #106; cross-user + workspace isolation suites stay green.
- M10 review fix-cycle (from the 2-agent `/review` of the M10 diff):
  - **F-arch-1 (HIGH) — cache correctness:** `recall({ scopes })` now BYPASSES the semantic recall cache (added `options?.scopes` to the `cacheApplicable` guard in `scoped-retrieval.ts`, mirroring `rerank`/`graphExpand`). Before, a wired cache both skipped the reweight on a HIT and stored the reweighted order under a scopes-blind key on a MISS — poisoning a later plain `recall()` and breaking the "default byte-identical" invariant. Regression test added (`factory-recall-cache.test.ts`).
  - **F-tests-2 (MEDIUM) — absent-recency neutrality:** the reweight now reads recency from the PROJECTED `metadata['created_at']` (not the `MemoryItem.createdAt` DTO field, which the pipeline back-fills with `now`). A legacy/stale-impl row with no projected timestamp is now NEUTRAL (recency 0), not a spurious MAX-recency boost that would bury genuinely fresh facts (the exact Risk #1). Unit test added (`test_absent_created_at_is_neutral_not_max_recency`).
  - **F-tests-4 (MEDIUM) — Inviolable Rule 9:** removed a vendor/paper name from a `src/**` test comment.
  - **F-arch-2 / F-tests-7 (MEDIUM) — API doc:** corrected the `RecallOptions.scopes` JSDoc `@throws` — combining with `rerank`/`diversify` throws `RerankerConflict` (not `InvalidScope`); `InvalidScope` is only for out-of-`[0,1]` weights.
  - **F-tests-5/6 (MEDIUM/LOW) — benchmark honesty:** reframed the recall@k doc/verdict as "surfaces the scope-preferred set into top-k" (the relevant set is the reweight's own target — not a scope-blind ground-truth win); and corrected the projection-cost arm to measure the marginal cost of M10's single added column `created_at` (`confirmation_count` was already projected pre-M10) → ≈ 0.27 ms.
  - **F-tests-3 (MEDIUM) — documented deviation:** the M10 recency signal is `created_at` only (not `last_accessed_at`); recorded as a deliberate KISS/YAGNI v1 choice in the benchmark doc.
  - **F-tests-1 (MEDIUM) — fail-soft:** documented that `scopeAwareReweight` is a TOTAL pure function (no throw path), so a `scope_reweight_failed` event/try-catch would be unreachable dead code — fail-soft is satisfied by construction (parsimony-ladder).
- **BEHAVIOR DISCLOSURE (M10 T1.1, F-arch-3):** `recall()` result DTOs now report the row's REAL `createdAt`/`updatedAt` (the projected `created_at`) instead of the recall-time `now`. This corrects a prior documented lie; ranking is unchanged (default order byte-identical), but callers reading `MemoryItem.createdAt` off a recall now see the true row age.

### Added
- M10 (scope-aware recall, T4.2): reproducible recall@k benchmark (`benchmarks/m10-scope-aware-recall.bench.mjs` + `docs/benchmarks/m10-scope-aware-recall.md`) proving the reweight does not regress ranking (ROADMAP M10 risk #2). On a uniform-cosine fixture (6 scope-preferred rows + 40 noise) the scope-aware reweight lifts recall@6 of the recent-session ∪ durable-global set to **1.000** vs a baseline **0.056** (mean over 3 runs) — the ranking is decided entirely by the weights. The two hot-path columns T1.1 added (`created_at`, `confirmation_count`) cost **≈ 0.31 ms** on a ~1 ms query (measured WITH vs WITHOUT the projection) — a real but sub-millisecond cost, accepted per blueprint ADR D3 (a second timestamp query would be an N+1, strictly worse).
- M10 (scope-aware recall, T4.1): the scope-aware ranking + cross-user isolation HARD GATE, proven end-to-end through `recall()` on real `ankane/pgvector` (`tests/integration/recall/scope-aware-ranking.test.ts`). A session recall with `RecallOptions.scopes` over the M8 `session ∪ global` union ranks fresh-session > stale-session (recency boost) and durable-global > one-off-global (`confirmation_count` stability boost); user B in the SAME workspace sees ZERO of user A's rows and only its own (the full `(workspace_id, user_id)` filter — ROADMAP M10 risk #1). A fixed unit-vector test embedder is used (not the shipped zero-vector `StubEmbedder`) so cosine is uniform across seeds and the reweight — not vector noise — is the sole ranking signal (a real, non-vacuous proof).
- M10 (scope-aware recall, T3.2): assert `attributedTo` (user/agent) survives the scope-aware reweight — the reweight is a `{...item, score}` spread, so every orthogonal field is carried to the recalled result (ROADMAP M10 DoD: attribution surfaced on each result). Unit: `pipeline.test.ts` +1 (`test_attribution_survives_scope_reweight`).
- M10 (scope-aware recall, T3.1): the opt-in `scopeAwareReweight` stage is now wired into the recall pipeline as a post-threshold rerank of the threshold-survivor pool, gated by `RecallOptions.scopes`. It is mutually exclusive with `rerank` and `diversify` (a combo throws `RerankerConflict` BEFORE any work), and an out-of-`[0,1]` weight throws `InvalidScope` at the boundary (matching the `diversify.lambda` precedent). The reweight reads each survivor's real `created_at`/`confirmation_count` (via `rowToItem`) so session-origin rows get a recency boost and global-origin rows a stability boost before top-K; default (no `scopes`) recall is byte-identical. A new OTel event `scope_reweight_applied` (`{scope_tier, reweighted_count}`) makes the ranking decision observable (Princípio 3; `MEMORY_EVENTS` 42 → 43). Isolation is unaffected — the reweight only re-orders an already-`(workspace,user)`-safe pool. Unit: `pipeline.test.ts` +4 (reorder-when-set, byte-identical-when-absent, guard, weight-validation).
- M10 (scope-aware recall, T1.2 + T2.1): `RecallOptions.scopes` opt-in (`{sessionRecencyWeight?, globalStabilityWeight?}`) + the pure deterministic `scopeAwareReweight` (`packages/core/src/domain/retrieval/scope-reweight.ts`). Over the M8 `session ∪ global` union, a session-origin row is boosted by recency (`1/(1+ageDays)`) and a global-origin row by stability (`count/(count+K)`), as a BOUNDED multiplier (`score*(1+weight*signal)`) that nudges without inverting a large base-score gap (Princípio 1). Pure (no I/O; `now` injected for determinism); default (no `scopes`) byte-identical. Wiring into the pipeline + attribution + the recall@k proof land in T3/T4. Unit: `scope-reweight.test.ts` 5/5 (recency/stability re-order, bounded, deterministic, zero-weights).

### Fixed
- M10 (scope-aware recall, T1.1): the vector `search` recall path now projects `created_at` (and carries it in the row payload) so a recalled row reports its REAL age instead of the recall-time `now` — the recency signal the M10 scope-aware reweight needs. Before this the `search` SQL `cols` omitted the timestamp, so the pipeline hardcoded `createdAt: now` and any recency boost was a silent no-op (the blueprint's load-bearing gap). Proven on real pgvector (`tests/integration/recall/created-at-projection.test.ts`). Legacy/stale-impl rows with no projected timestamp still fall back to `now` (never a silent 1970 lie).
- M9 (session→global consolidation) `/review` fixes: (1) a lone durable session fact (3 exact mentions dedup to ONE `confirmation_count=3` row — the literal DoD scenario) now promotes — the reflect consolidation phase runs with ≥1 source memory for the M9 policy (individual promotion), not the v0.7 ≥2 pair-merge gate; (2) `persistConsolidations` returns the count of rows ACTUALLY promoted and writes NO audit row / emits NO `consolidation_promoted` metric for an idempotent re-run or a concurrency-loser (Princípio 3 fidelity — no over-report); (3) EC-8 atomicity (rollback), true concurrency (exactly-one-flip), undefined/boundary confirmation_count, and the real `remember()`×3 dedup→promote chain are now proven on real pgvector; (4) `SESSION_TO_GLOBAL_KIND` shared const so the orchestrator gate and policy cannot drift. Plan/blueprint/ROADMAP reconciled to the shipped individual-promotion + dedup-to-one + `consolidation_history`-provenance reality (no overclaim). Verdict READY_TO_MERGE.

### Changed
- Internal (cycle tooling): the `/code-quality` D2 symbol-fabrication detector (`.claude/skills/code-quality/scripts/detectors/typescript.py`) now recognizes ALL pnpm-workspace members (root + `packages/*`), not just the outermost package name — a sibling import (`@usetheo/memory-api`, `-mcp`, `-agent-core`, resolved via the `workspace:` protocol, not on the public npm registry) is treated as LOCAL, not a fabricated npm package. Fixes a repo-wide false-positive `FAIL_HARD` that flagged every integration-test slice importing a sibling package. No product-code change.

### Added
- M9 (session→global consolidation, T3.1 + T4): the `reflect` boundary now PROMOTES durable session memories to global — when a `SessionToGlobalConsolidationPolicy` is passed as `reflect({ conflictResolution })` on a `session` scope, the reflect-orchestrator resolves the session's candidate cluster (now carrying `confirmation_count`), runs the policy, and calls `persistConsolidations({ promote: true })`. A new OTel event `consolidation_promoted` (`{scope_tier, promoted_count}`) makes every promotion observable (Princípio 3, wiring pillar c). Proven end-to-end on real pgvector (`tests/integration/consolidation/session-to-global.test.ts`, 2/2): a durable session fact (confirmation_count ≥ 3) is promoted to global with a `consolidation_history` audit row; a one-off session fact is NOT promoted (stays session-scoped, decays via eviction); and another user in the same workspace sees NONE of it (the `(workspace, user)` isolation hard gate). Honest scope: v1 promotes by the exact-dedup `confirmation_count` signal (Princípio 1) — each durable memory promoted individually, distinct facts NEVER rejected as duplicates (cosine-cluster promotion of paraphrases is a future enhancement).
- M9 (session→global consolidation, T2.1): `PgvectorStore.persistConsolidations` gains an optional `{ promote?: boolean }` — when `true`, the durable representative is PROMOTED to global (`session_id→NULL, agent_id→NULL`) in the SAME transaction as the duplicate soft-delete + `consolidation_history` audit, so promotion + decay + provenance are atomic (EC-8 rollback parity). The promotion NEVER touches `workspace_id`/`user_id` (the M9 isolation hard gate) and is idempotent (a re-run updates 0 rows via the `session_id IS NOT NULL OR agent_id IS NOT NULL` guard). Backward-compatible: the v0.7 distillation path (no `promote`) keeps the kept row session-scoped. Proven on real pgvector (`tests/integration/consolidation/promote-to-global.test.ts`, 3/3).
- M9 (session→global consolidation, T1.1): `SessionToGlobalConsolidationPolicy` — a `ConflictResolutionPolicy` impl (reuses the shipped DIP) that decides promotion of a durable session cluster (representative's `confirmation_count ≥ threshold`, default 3, grounded in Princípio 1 — NOT a copied reference pattern) to the user's global scope. Pure decision (no I/O); keeps the durable representative, rejects the cluster duplicates, carries `originalEpisodeIds` lineage. Exported from `@usetheo/memory/contract`. `ConsolidationCandidate` gains an OPTIONAL `confirmationCount` (backward-compatible; the v0.7 distillation policies ignore it). Persistence (`session_id→NULL`) + reflect-boundary wiring land in T2/T3.

## [0.39.0] - 2026-07-22

### Added
- Fast unit test `scope-filters.test.ts` for `filtersFromScope` (7 cases: user/session/agent WITH userId bind the composite key; session/agent WITHOUT userId are workspace-scoped admin path; repo→agent_id; unbound workspace) — the security-critical filter shape now has contract-suite coverage, not only the env-gated integration test (M8 review F-tests-1)
- Benchmark `benchmarks/m8-isolation-filter.bench.mjs` + results (`docs/benchmarks/m8-isolation-filter.md`): the `(workspace,user,session)` isolation filter is 0.582ms±0.078 WITH the composite index (Index Scan) vs 0.685ms±0.097 WITHOUT (Bitmap Heap Scan), 20k rows / 5 runs on real pgvector — 1.18x, and the composite index avoids the heap recheck that grows with scale (M8 T4.3)
- ADR-0021: M8 `(workspace, user)` isolation is boundary-enforced (optional `userId` bound from `Principal.userId` at scope resolution), superseding the plan's type-mandatory D1 — records the owner `/goal` sign-off + the design rationale (nullable `Principal.userId` for workspace-admin) (M8 T5.1)
- Composite btree index `memories_ws_user_session_idx` on `(workspace_id, user_id, session_id) WHERE t_invalid IS NULL` (migration 0024) — backs the M8 `(workspace, user[, session])` isolation filter; EXPLAIN confirms an Index Scan on the composite key (was single-column-only, the at-scale bottleneck per the blueprint) (M8 T3.1). Drizzle `0024_snapshot.json` added so the `drizzle-kit generate` chain stays valid (M8 review F-dom-db-1). NOTE (F-dom-db-2, deferred): the index is NOT mirrored as a `schema.ts` object because that file exceeds the 300-LoC quality budget — `drizzle-kit push` dev-parity is deferred to a schema.ts split refactor; the runtime + `runMigrations` path is unaffected (the migrator reads the `.sql`, not the drizzle snapshot). The migration is created WITHOUT `CONCURRENTLY` (F-arch-2/F-xval-2): drizzle's `runMigrations` wraps each migration in a transaction and `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` is forbidden inside a txn — the tradeoff (a brief write-lock on first deploy over the existing row count) is accepted for the M8 index; a zero-downtime `CONCURRENTLY` rebuild is an ops runbook step if the table is already large at deploy time
- REST `remember`/`recall` now resolve the user tier from the edge-injected `X-Theo-User-Id` when the body names none, matching the MCP surface — so gateway-fronted callers (the Claude Code kit hooks) land under the credential's user, unifying REST and MCP memory in the space the dashboard reads (theo-cloud#249)
- Roadmap amended: added M8 `(workspace, user)` isolation model + agent-chosen session↔global routing (`/roadmap-feature intelligent-memory-routing`)
- Roadmap amended: added M9 Session → global consolidation (within the same `(workspace, user)`) (`/roadmap-feature intelligent-memory-routing`)
- Roadmap amended: added M10 Scope-aware recall (global + current session union, filtered by `(workspace, user)`) (`/roadmap-feature intelligent-memory-routing`)
- Roadmap amended: added M11 Routing governance & observability (per-`(workspace,user)` config + audit + dashboard) (`/roadmap-feature intelligent-memory-routing`)

### Changed
- Internal (M8 code-quality cleanup): fixed import order in `local-skill-factory.ts` and made `recallTraceDeps` module-private in `scoped-retrieval.ts` (intra-file use only — no orphan export). Lint 0 warnings, typecheck 0 errors.
- M8 routing (review F-xval-1): the MCP `remember`/`recall` tools now let the agent (Claude Code) choose per call between durable GLOBAL memory `(workspace, user)` (omit `sessionId`) and an ephemeral SESSION sub-partition `(workspace, user, session)` (pass `sessionId`) — via the new `routeScopedMemory` helper (`packages/mcp/src/tools/route-scoped-memory.ts`). The user identity ALWAYS comes from the auth-trusted `ScopedContext` (never a tool argument), so the agent can only pick a session sub-partition of its OWN user space, never address another user. Proven end-to-end against real pgvector (`tests/integration/mcp/routing.test.ts`: a session-scoped recall returns ONLY that session's fact, not the global one) + unit-tested (`route-scoped-memory.test.ts`, 3 cases). This narrows ADR D3 ("agents MUST NOT pass scope") to allow the `sessionId` sub-partition while keeping `userId`/`workspaceId` transport-bound
- M8 hierarchical scope recall (owner decision 2026-07-22, system-wide tier semantics): the three memory tiers now form a HIERARCHY instead of disjoint partitions, via a new `Filters.scopeHierarchy`. A GLOBAL (User-tier) recall is GLOBAL-ONLY — `session_id IS NULL AND agent_id IS NULL` — so ephemeral session/agent working memory never leaks into the durable space; a user-bound SESSION recall is SESSION ∪ GLOBAL — `agent_id IS NULL AND (session_id = :session OR session_id IS NULL)` — so inside a session the agent recalls its durable global knowledge PLUS the session context. Applies to EVERY consumer of the User/Session tiers (REST `GET /v1/scopes/user/:id`, hooks, SDK, MCP) — one contract. The workspace-admin path (Session/Agent scope with no acting user) is unchanged (exact workspace-tier filter); Agent/Repo tiers keep plain equality. `session_id` stays in the filter for the WRITE column derivation; `scopeHierarchy` drives only the RECALL predicate (both stores skip the plain session_id/agent_id equality when it is present). Proven against real pgvector (`tests/integration/mcp/routing.test.ts`: global recall excludes the session fact; session recall includes both) + unit (`scope-filters.test.ts`); zero regressions across the 1227-test suite. This resolves the F-xval-1 routing fork
- SECURITY (M8 review F-arch-1): the REST **user tier** now binds to the auth-trusted `Principal.userId` on BOTH the write path (`resolveScope` — `POST /v1/{remember,recall,reflect}`) and the read path (`pathParamsToScoped` — `GET /v1/scopes/user/{id}/*`), so a user-Principal can no longer read/write another user's global memory by putting `{userId:'victim'}` in the body or `.../user/victim` in the path — the client-supplied id is ignored when an acting user is present (cross-user IDOR closed). A workspace-admin credential (`Principal.userId` null) keeps the body/path id (no acting user to bind). Proven at the HTTP boundary by `cross-user-rest-isolation.test.ts` (4 cases, two Principals)
- REST scope resolution now binds the authenticated `Principal.userId` into path/body-addressed session & agent scopes (all handlers: scopes, entities, scoped-forget, episodes, metrics + remember/recall/reflect via `resolveScope`) — so `/v1/scopes/session/{id}` and a body-named session write are `(workspace, user, session)`, closing the cross-user leak; a workspace-admin credential (Principal.userId null) stays workspace-scoped (M8 ADR D6)
- `Memory.session(sessionId, userId?)` / `Memory.agent(agentId, userId?)` gain an OPTIONAL `userId` that binds the scope to a user — `(workspace, user, session)` when supplied, workspace-scoped when omitted (backward-compatible; 1-arg form unchanged). The isolation boundary is enforced at the scope-resolution boundary (ADR D6), not by a type-breaking change — a `Principal.userId`-null workspace-admin credential stays workspace-scoped (M8)
- Internal: split `local-memory.ts` (377 LoC) — skill-factory methods to `local-skill-factory.ts`, deduped the repeated `LocalScopedMemory` construction into a `scoped()` helper — so no module exceeds the 300-LoC budget (M8 T0-b, pure refactor)
- Internal: split `scoped-memory.ts` (602 LoC) into `scope-filters.ts` + `row-mapping.ts` + `scoped-retrieval.ts` so no module exceeds the 300-LoC budget and the duplicated row-mapping block is deduped — pure refactor, helpers re-exported for backward compat, behavior byte-identical (M8 T0, unblocks the (workspace, user) isolation work)
- Claude Code kit `remember`/`precompact` hooks now write in a DETACHED background worker (`postDetached`) instead of a blocking `await`: server-side LLM fact-extraction takes ~8s through the gateway, so the previous 3s-timeout `await` silently dropped every write while still risking an 8s session stall — the hook now returns in ~0.03s and the write lands under the unified user subject once extraction completes (theo-cloud#249)
- Claude Code kit `recall` hook omits `userId` by default so the gateway's edge-injected `X-Theo-User-Id` drives the tier, reading the account's one unified memory space (the space the dashboard shows) instead of a per-project pseudo-user (theo-cloud#249)

### Deprecated

### Removed

### Fixed
- MCP tool calls ignored the edge-resolved user and always used the per-deployment `THEOMEM_MCP_DEFAULT_USER_ID`, so an agent's memories landed under an identity no consumer queries — the dashboard reads `/v1/scopes/user/{session-subject}/memories` and found nothing (theo-cloud#249)

### Security

## [0.38.0] - 2026-07-21

### Added
- Claude Code kit can target the Theo gateway via `THEOMEM_MEMORY_PATH_PREFIX`, instead of hardcoding theo-memory's own REST paths (M7)
- Edge-contract regression suite for the MCP gateway: fail-closed without/with a forged credential, gateway-not-static-file-server, handshake, tools exposure, concurrent clients, unknown-session rejection, body-claimed-tenant, cross-tenant isolation (M7)
- MCP upstream service (`themory mcp`, streamable-http + TLS + `--auth api-keys`) in the dev deploy, so the Theo MCP Gateway has something to front at `/mcp/memory` (M7)
- Roadmap amended: added M7 Claude Code ⇄ theo-memory via the Theo MCP Gateway (dev, SOTA-level) (`/roadmap-feature claude-code-mcp-gateway-dev`) — corrects the integration architecture M6 shipped, after M6's own risk #1 ("production edge host unconfirmed → silent 404") materialized: the MCP had been registered as stdio (DB + LLM credentials on every client) and the hooks pointed at an unauthenticated public endpoint.


### Fixed
- `/release` derived the next version with `git describe --tags`, which returns the nearest tag by ancestry; since release tags live on `main`, it read v0.10.0 while the latest release was v0.37.0 and would have cut a version backwards (M7)
- MCP session eviction hung on `finish`, which never fires when a client aborts mid-flight, so an aborted handshake still leaked its server until shutdown; it now evicts on `close`, which covers both endings (M7)
- Claude Code kit ignored `THEOMEM_MEMORY_PATH_PREFIX` when set in `~/.claude/theo-memory.env` — the documented gateway setup was a silent no-op that kept sending the client credential straight to the service (M7)
- MCP sessions that never completed a handshake were only released at shutdown, so every sessionless request pinned a server for the process lifetime; they are now evicted when their response ends (M7)
- MCP concurrency tests ran sequentially despite their name, so they never exercised two sessions opening at once; they are now genuinely parallel and a ten-client fan-out case was added (M7)
- Edge cross-tenant test reported a false isolation failure when both credentials belonged to the same workspace; it now separates that misconfiguration from a real leak (M7)
- Claude Code kit documented the gateway as a URL suffix, which produced `/v1/memory/v1/recall`; and the dev deploy could report success over a crash-looping MCP upstream (M7)
- MCP graceful shutdown was a no-op over HTTP: the listener handle was discarded and SIGTERM stopped a detached server, so every container recreate cut in-flight requests (M7)
- MCP sessions created before `initialize` completed were unreachable by teardown, leaking one server per abandoned handshake (M7)
- MCP shutdown closed the HTTP listener before its sessions, which could hang forever on a live SSE stream (M7)
- Entity labels could come back half-translated (a Portuguese input yielded `engineer mecânico`), breaking the source-language guarantee; the extraction prompt now forbids mixing languages inside a compound label (#103)
- The dev deploy only redeployed the REST API, leaving the MCP upstream on an older build of the same image; both are now pulled, recreated, and version-gated together (M7)
- `/code-quality` wrote its audit to a hardcoded knowledge-base root and reported `report_path: null` (it was set after the JSON was serialized); the audit now lands beside the plan it audited and the path is present in the summary (M7)
- `/code-quality` could not run on this repo: its languages config was written in a one-per-line format no consumer reads, and plan resolution only looked under `.claude/knowledge-base/` while this repo keeps `knowledge-base/` at the root (M7)
- MCP streamable-http accepted only ONE client: a singleton transport made every client after the first fail with `-32600 "Server already initialized"`. Sessions are now per-client (transport + server per `Mcp-Session-Id`), so every Claude Code instance can connect (M7)
- **M7 Phase 1 — Claude Code ⇄ theo-memory auth + MCP routing unblocked on dev.** Fixed a split-brain credential store (the dashboard minted into the k3d Postgres while the ForwardAuth validator read an empty compose database, so every `theo_live_*` returned 401) and exposed the Theo MCP Gateway at the public edge (`/mcp/*` had no Caddy `handle` block and fell through to the SPA file_server). A valid key now authenticates, and `POST /mcp/memory` without a credential returns a gated `401 application/json` instead of HTML. Remaining: theo-memory's admin API was unmounted (closed since — see Security), so Model B per-tenant minting 404s — deliberately deferred behind closing issue #101 (that service is publicly unauthenticated; mounting key minting on it first would be worse). (claude-code-mcp-gateway-dev)
- **`flip_milestone_checkbox.py` never flipped a milestone.** Its header regex required `###`, while the format documented in `cycle-release.md` / `cycle-roadmap.md` (and emitted by `/roadmap-init`) is `## M<N> — [ ] <name>`. Every release against a spec-conformant `ROADMAP.md` therefore hit the "milestone not found" WARN path and skipped the flip silently — the checkbox never moved and the roadmap-runs audit file was never written. The pattern now accepts `#{2,3}`. Found while closing M5 + M6 for v0.37.0 (the first release where the flip was actually exercised).


### Security
- theo-memory accepted unauthenticated reads AND writes from any internet host on the dev deploy; the service now fails closed on a missing credential and the key-lifecycle API is mounted only behind an explicit bootstrap token (#101)
- MCP tool calls dropped the auth-trusted workspace: `withScope` replaces the ambient context, so binding the user tier discarded the `workspaceId` resolved from the Principal. With a default user id configured (as the deployed MCP service has), every tenant's tool calls ran unbound against shared rows (M7)

## [0.37.0] - 2026-07-21

### Added
- **M5 — CI hardening (Phase 2).** `.github/workflows/integration.yml` (pgvector service on an ephemeral host port + build + schema provisioning + `test:integration` as a required gate, on the self-hosted runner — the ~95-test integration suite previously ran only locally and `skipIf`-skipped without a DB) + `.github/workflows/security-sast.yml` (semgrep TypeScript/OWASP via `docker run`, SHA-pinned `uses:`, scoped to the production TS surface `packages` with test files excluded). Completes the M5 CD-pipeline milestone; the deferred `/v1/version` + `/v1/healthz{,/ready}` aliases shipped via the M6 `app.ts` SRP refactor (cd-pipeline-dev).
- **M6 — Claude Code memory migration tool + global kit.** `scripts/migrate-claude-code-memory.mjs` (stdlib-only) migrates Claude Code's native file-memory (`~/.claude/projects/*/memory/*.md`, both frontmatter variants + `[[wikilinks]]`) into theo-memory: a MANDATORY fail-closed secret/PII scrub (private-key blocks / OpenAI / `theo_live_*` / AWS / JWT / GitHub / high-entropy → `[REDACTED]`; a node >40% redacted or with a private-key block is SKIPPED, never sent) → `infer:false` verbatim import via `/v1/remember/batch` with `metadata:{source:'claude-code', project, file, node_type}`. Default DRY-RUN (review report; `--commit` to persist). Real-corpus dry-run: 411 nodes, 107 redacted, 0 leaked (a 3-layer test asserts a planted secret never reaches the HTTP payload). Plus the version-controlled global kit `docs/claude-code-kit/` — SessionStart auto-recall + Stop auto-remember + PreCompact hooks (deterministic, memory-outage never blocks a session) + an idempotent `~/.claude/settings.json` merge installer + remote-MCP registration (claude-code-memory-integration).
- **M6 — `POST /v1/remember/batch` (batch import).** One request carries an array of per-item remember payloads (validated by `BatchRememberBody`, bounded at 1000); each item reuses the exact `scoped.remember` path (`infer:false` verbatim store + composite-UPSERT idempotency), so a re-run bumps `confirmation_count`/`rejectedAsDuplicate` (no double-insert) and the aggregate reports `{imported, deduped, failed, errors?}`. `memory:write`-scoped, workspace from the Principal. One request = N facts = one write-limiter slot, so migrating thousands of pre-written facts is feasible against the 30-writes/60s limit. Integration-tested vs real pgvector (import N once, provenance `metadata.source` round-trips on recall, idempotent re-run) + a per-item-failure isolation unit test (a poison item is reported, never fails the whole batch). Shipped alongside an `app.ts` route-registration SRP refactor (427→168 LoC) that also landed the deferred `/v1/version` + `/v1/healthz{,/ready}` probe aliases (claude-code-memory-integration).
- Roadmap amended: added M5 CD pipeline — 100% functional in dev (`/roadmap-feature cd-pipeline-dev`)
- Roadmap amended: added M6 Global Claude Code ⇄ theo-memory integration + migration (`/roadmap-feature claude-code-memory-integration`); grounded in the deep-research blueprint `claude-code-theo-memory-integration` (hooks-first hybrid; `POST /v1/remember/batch`; secret-scrubbed idempotent migration of Claude Code's native file-memory)
- **M5 CD pipeline — dev auto-deploy (playbook Phase 3-4).** `publish.yml` gains a `deploy-dev` job (develop-only; a `v*` tag publishes the release image but never deploys) that SSHes to the dev host, syncs the deploy mirror to the pushed commit, logs into GHCR, and runs the new `scripts/deploy-dev.sh` — which pulls `ghcr.io/usetheodev/themory:develop`, brings up `themory_app` alongside the existing pgvector via the new `docker-compose.deploy.yml`, then gates the deploy on the image's OCI revision label == pushed sha (version gate) AND `/v1/health` + `/v1/health/db` green (health gate). `secrets: inherit` → explicit block (semgrep). (`/v1/version` + `/v1/healthz{,/ready}` aliases deferred within M5 pending an `app.ts` SRP refactor.)


### Fixed
- **Standalone compose files were flat-indented / unparseable — fixed (M5 blocker).** `docker-compose.yaml` + `docker-compose.prod.yaml` had every service property at the service-NAME indentation level (1-space flat), so a strict `docker compose config` rejected them (`services.restart must be a mapping`) — the M5 dev-deploy merge (`-f docker-compose.yaml -f docker-compose.deploy.yml`) failed on the host. Re-indented both to valid YAML (same content — container names + named volumes preserved, so a running pgvector is not recreated). Also corrected the stale `ghcr.io/usetheo` → `ghcr.io/usetheodev` coordinate in the prod-compose comment.
- **`publish` CI red fixed — registered the `@usetheo/memory/recall-trace-pg` subpath in `scripts/rewrite-dist-aliases.mjs`.** The recall-trace work (S2/M4) added the subpath export imported by the dist server + MCP entrypoints, but the fail-loud postbuild alias-rewrite map was not updated, so `pnpm build` aborted (`UNRESOLVED specifier @usetheo/memory/recall-trace-pg`) → the docker image build failed → no image shipped (all recent `publish` runs red). Adds the one alias entry; `pnpm build` green (145 specifiers rewritten). (M5 CD pipeline — task 1.)
