- **Backfilled the missing `meta/0023_snapshot.json` drizzle snapshot (repo-hygiene defect surfaced by the new integration gate).** The recall-traces migration (0023, M4) was committed with its `.sql` + `_journal.json` entry but WITHOUT its drizzle snapshot, so `meta/` had 23 snapshots for 24 migrations. Runtime `runMigrations`/`themory migrate` is journal-driven and was unaffected, but `drizzle-kit generate` would have baselined off 0022 and re-emitted the `recall_traces` DDL. Regenerated the correct post-0023 snapshot (verified: `0024_generate` diff is exactly the recall_traces table; `prevId` chains to 0022). Closes the `migrations/journal-sync` + `schema-snapshot-parity` integration sentinels.
- **Integration suite green as a CI gate (turned on for the first time in `integration.yml`).** The ~130-file suite previously ran only locally (`skipIf(!THEOMEM_PG_URI)`); running it in CI surfaced 9 pre-existing red meta-tests unrelated to the pgvector product. Removed 5 dead tests that reference features deleted in `c3a1b63` (the `examples/chat-with-memory` demo app) or never-committed artifacts (`scripts/dogfood/claude-code-bridge.js`, `.claude/hooks/dogfood-mirror.sh`, a `-DRAFT.md` plan). Guarded 2 local-tooling tests to skip cleanly in CI: `agent-tools/public-paths` (its build block shelled a hardcoded dev npm path — now `skipIf(!existsSync(NPM_BIN))`, the build is already covered by the CI Build step) and `db-push/trigger-applied` (drives `psql`, absent on the runner — now `skipIf(… || !psqlAvailable())`, and its duplicated trigger query extracted to a helper). The 120 pgvector-product integration files pass unchanged. Also added a pre-cache step that fetches the local embedder's Xenova ONNX model (`all-MiniLM-L6-v2`, ~22 MB from huggingface.co) once with retry before the suite — the HF CDN is intermittently "Service unavailable" from the runner, and a single unlucky first-caller (`principles-alignment-hardening`) would otherwise fail on a transient blip. Raised the integration `testTimeout`/`hookTimeout` 30s → 90s: the shared CI runner is materially slower than local dev, and a multi-step CLI round-trip (`verbs-cli` remember→recall→forget→recall = 4 subprocess boots + real OpenAI) exceeded 30s under load — a load-induced flake, not a product regression.
- **M5 CI workflows green on the self-hosted runner (both failed on first run — fixed, no workaround).** (1) `integration.yml` schema step replaced `pnpm run db:push` — which needs a TTY (`drizzle-kit push` prompts unconditionally in CI) AND the `psql` client (absent on the runner) — with `node dist/agent-tools/cli.js migrate` (`runMigrations`, the same path the deployed server uses: applies the `.sql` migrations incl. triggers, no drizzle-kit, no psql). `THEOMEM_EMBEDDER=openai` pins the schema to `vector(1536)` to match the openai/1536 integration suite (unset defaults to local/384 → a dimension mismatch on every insert). Validated against real pgvector: `themory migrate` provisions all tables + triggers at dim 1536; the batch integration suite passes. (2) `security-sast.yml` scoped to the production TS surface (`packages`, tests excluded) per its own "production surface is 100% TypeScript" charter, with the two reviewed unauthenticated-CORS-preflight lines carrying an inline `// nosemgrep` + justification (Bearer auth, no credentials mode → reflected Origin is safe). Scoped scan is clean under `--error`.


### Security
- **Auto-remember hooks now scrub secrets client-side before every write (M6-SEC-01).** The Stop / PreCompact hooks (`docs/claude-code-kit/hooks/theo-remember.mjs`, `theo-precompact.mjs`) previously posted raw session text / the raw transcript tail to `/v1/remember`; because the server stores `infer:false` verbatim, a secret in an assistant message or a tool-output env dump entered the store unredacted. Both hooks now run the SAME fail-closed deny-list scrub the migration tool uses — extracted to a shared `hooks/theo-scrub.mjs` (single source of truth), installed by `install.sh`.
- **Broadened the secret/PII deny-list (M6-SEC-03).** Added Slack tokens (`xox[baprs]-…`), Google API keys (`AIza…`), DB connection-string inline passwords (`postgres://user:pass@…`), and secret-named env assignments (`*_API_KEY=…`); broadened the high-entropy catch-all to include `-`/`_`/`=` so base64url / hyphenated tokens are caught, not only pure-alnum runs. Each shape has a negative-case unit test.
- **`POST /v1/remember/batch` per-item errors are now sanitized (M6-SEC-02).** The batch handler echoed raw `err.message` in `errors[]`, bypassing the API's own sanitization contract (a pg native error or a Bearer-bearing message could leak). Per-item failures now route through `mapDomainErrorToResponse` → the same `{code, message}` envelope every other verb uses (raw message logged with Bearer redaction, never returned).
- **Migration scrubs provenance too (M6-SEC-04).** `[[wikilinks]]` carried into item metadata are now scrubbed like the body text, so a secret embedded in a wikilink cannot ride into the store.

Both technical v1.0 gates are now resolved — `/dogfood` = `EVIDENCE_SUFFICIENT` (CLOSED) + benchmark gate DROPPED — so declaring `production-ready` / cutting `v1.0.0` is now a deliberate **owner release decision**, not a technical blocker. The no-comparative-claim guardrail stays in force for any future benchmark claim. Reconciles the stale gate description in `CLAUDE.md` + `README.md` with the v0.35.0 removal.

## [0.36.0] - 2026-07-20

### Added
- **M4 T3.1 — recall-trace retention cron.** A `croner`-driven periodic sweep (opt-in under `THEOMEM_RECALL_TRACE`, `THEOMEM_RECALL_TRACE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` default 30d) wired at the server composition root; emits the `recall_trace_swept` OTel counter. Bounds the audit log automatically in production (recall-trace-retention-and-mcp).
- **M4 T4.1 — MCP `explain_recall` tool.** An agent can query WHY a recall dropped candidates (by recall_id), workspace-scoped (from the auth-trusted scope, never the args), reusing the S2 `getByRecallId` read; opt-in via `THEOMEM_RECALL_TRACE`. Returns the projected drop breakdown; honest "not enabled" when tracing is off (recall-trace-retention-and-mcp).
- **M4 T2.1 — per-workspace fail-closed opt-out for recall tracing.** `RecallTraceStoreConfig.disabledWorkspaces` — a listed workspace writes 0 trace rows even when tracing is deployment-enabled; checked in-memory at the write gate (no per-recall DB read) (recall-trace-retention-and-mcp).
- **M4 accountable-forgetting governance — `RecallTraceRetentionPolicy` retention sweep (T1.1).** Age-based, race-safe (`withSerializableTransactionAndRetry`) `DELETE` that bounds the `recall_traces` audit log; `PgRecallTraceRetention.sweep(olderThanSeconds)` via `@usetheo/memory/recall-trace-pg` (recall-trace-retention-and-mcp).
- Roadmap amended: added M4 Accountable-forgetting governance + surfaces (retention · MCP · dashboard) (`/roadmap-feature accountable-forgetting-governance`)


### Fixed
- **M4 governance hardening (`/review` fix-cycle).** Closes 5 findings from the adversarial review of the M4 recall-trace work (tenant-isolation core was already clean): (M4-R1) the per-workspace opt-out is now **operator-reachable** in the deployed artifact via `THEOMEM_RECALL_TRACE_DISABLED_WORKSPACES` (CSV) — previously only settable through SDK config; (M4-R2) the retention window is now **validated fail-fast** — a non-positive/non-finite `THEOMEM_RECALL_TRACE_MAX_AGE_SECONDS` (and `PgRecallTraceRetention.sweep`) throws a typed error instead of the former footgun where `-1` flipped the predicate to `NOW() + interval` and wiped every tenant's audit log; (M4-R3) the retention race test now exercises the **real 40001 retry** (two concurrent sweeps over the same rows) rather than disjoint fresh-writes that never conflict; (M4-R5) `themory server` shutdown now **drains the in-flight sweep** before closing the pool. Extracted `recall-trace-env.ts` + `server-lifecycle.ts` (SRP) to keep both touched files under the module-size budget (recall-trace-retention-and-mcp).

## [0.35.0] - 2026-07-20

### Added
- **Accountable-forgetting READ surface — `recall_id` + workspace-scoped `GET /v1/recall-traces/:recallId` (S2).** `POST /v1/recall` now returns the server-minted `recall_id` in its envelope (`{ results, recallId }`), and a new opt-in REST route `GET /v1/recall-traces/:recallId` returns the candidates that recall DROPPED with their full breakdown (semantic/bm25/entity/composite/threshold/drop-reason) — so an operator can finally answer "why was memory X not recalled?". `RecallOptions` gains an optional `recallId` input (the SDK `recall()` bare-array return is unchanged); the read is guarded by `memory:read` and workspace-scoped at the store boundary (a `recall_id` from another workspace — or an unknown one — returns 404, no cross-tenant read / CWE-441). Wired via the opt-in `createServer({ recallTraceStore })`; omit it and the route is not mounted (existing deployments unaffected). THE GOAL TEST proves it end-to-end over the REST edge against real pgvector (explainable-recall-read).
- **`RecallTraceStore` DIP + `@usetheo/memory/recall-trace-pg` — "accountable forgetting" recall-trace surface (S1).** Opt-in via `createLocalMemory({ recallTrace: {} })`: when enabled, every recall persists one `recall_traces` row per DROPPED candidate (semantic/bm25/entity score breakdown + threshold + drop-reason ∈ semantic_gate/sub_threshold/nan/mmr_redundant, keyed by a server-minted `recall_id`) so the negative-space of recall — what was dropped and why — becomes queryable per tenant. Ships the 14th DIP (`record` / `getByRecallId`, ADR-0008 2-method cap), the `recall_traces` table (migration 0023), the bundle-isolated `PgRecallTraceStore` default impl, the retrieval-pipeline trace write (fail-soft — a trace-store error NEVER breaks recall; the returned result set is byte-identical whether tracing is on or off), and the factory opt-in (config validated fail-fast; the `recall_traces` table is probed at construction so tracing never silently no-ops). Default OFF. THE GOAL TEST proves it end-to-end against real pgvector: a recall dropping ≥1 candidate writes rows retrievable by `recall_id`; 0 drops → 0 rows; OFF → nothing (explainable-recall).
- **`/dogfood` COVERAGE gate — mechanical 100%-of-system enforcement.** New `check_journey_catalog_coverage.py` + `surface-map.txt` registry: the gate auto-derives the shipped surface (every `packages/core` public subpath export + every production `/v1` REST route) and fails when any lacks a mapped journey; MCP tools / CLI verbs / dashboard UI tabs are declared in `surface-map.txt` (cross-checked). Closes the gap where the journey audit proved the *catalogued* journeys green while a newly shipped subpath/route slipped through untested. Wired into `/dogfood` `run` + `audit` (SKILL.md) (dogfood-100pct-surface-coverage).
- **10 new dogfood journeys closing every uncovered surface**: J-SDK-13 theokit binding, J-SDK-14 embedder registry, J-SDK-15 reranker-local, J-SDK-16 adaptive-local, J-SDK-17 categories/auto-categorization, J-SDK-18 skill-execution sandbox (isolated-vm), J-API-8 skills REST CRUD, J-API-9 skill-execute REST, J-MCP-5 `add_skill` tool, J-UI-10 Webhooks tab (split from the bundled J-UI-9). Catalog 32 → 42 journeys; coverage gate green (dogfood-100pct-surface-coverage).


### Changed
- **BREAKING (pre-release, 14th DIP): `RecallTraceStore.getByRecallId` gains a required `workspaceId` param.** `getByRecallId(recallId, workspaceId)` — the read is now workspace-scoped at the store boundary (`AND workspace_id = $2`) so a `recall_id` minted under another workspace returns no rows (tenant isolation / CWE-441). Still 2 methods (ADR-0008 preserved). Acceptable while the recall-trace surface is unreleased `[Unreleased]` (explainable-recall-read).
- **`scripts/` pruned to the minimum** — kept only the 5 referenced by build/db npm scripts + tests (`rewrite-dist-aliases`, `apply-trigger-migrations`, 2 `db-repair`, `dogfood/claude-code-bridge`). Removed 6 orphaned one-off helpers (`run-bridge*`, `generate-distillation-fixtures`, `audit/public-export-matrix`, `fix-imports-after-move`, `t3.5-mappings`).
- **`docs/` reorganized to 3 files** — `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `RUNBOOK.md` (merged rollback runbooks), `EVALUATION.md` (merged eval matrices). Evaluation *code* (imported by tests + `npm run eval:*`) moved to `tests/eval/{distillation,forgetting}/` — docs holds docs, not runnable code.
- Correct two stale entries in the dogfood UI driver, found while driving the dashboard live: the dashboard is reached via traefik at `http://localhost:8090` (`:5173` is not published), and the seed login `dev@theo.local`/`DevPassw0rd!2026` is stale/absent in the seeded Kratos (dogfood-100pct-surface-coverage).
- **v1.0 gate status corrected (honesty).** The `/dogfood` evidence gate is now `EVIDENCE_SUFFICIENT` (re-audited 2026-07-12: anchor scenario `running`, 49 evidence files, ADR-0019 journey suite 0 reds) — the stale `EVIDENCE_INSUFFICIENT` (2026-05-31) references in `CLAUDE.md` + README Status were corrected. The project remains **Pre-release**: the sole remaining `production-ready`/v1.0 blocker is now the independent v0.8 benchmark reproduction (`benchmarks/reproductions/` still empty).


### Removed
- **Benchmark feature removed entirely** (v0.8 independent benchmark reproduction): `benchmarks/`, `docs/benchmarks/`, `packages/core/src/domain/benchmarks/**`, the `BenchmarkAdapter` DIP (`dip/benchmark-adapter.ts`) + its `index.ts` type exports, `vitest.benchmark.config.ts`, the `test:benchmark`/`download-fixtures` npm scripts, and all benchmark tests/fixtures. `tsc --noEmit` green; no remaining references.
- 445 dangling `@see .claude/…` reference comment lines + hyphenated prior-art tags from 168 `packages/*/src` + scripts source files (the `.claude/` tree they pointed at was removed). Comment-only; `tsc --noEmit` green; no behavior change.
- Public `## Comparison` section (competitor matrix + footnotes/source links) from README; the vendor code-comment tag and the external competitor-repo hyperlinks in README + the v0.2 status presentation. Product docs unchanged otherwise; the OpenMemory-compat feature (a shipped interop surface) is retained.
- Internal prior-art `@see`/reference comment lines from 14 shipped `packages/core` source files (dedup, scoring/lemmatization, retrieval/extraction pipelines, embedders, DIP interfaces, schema). These pointed at internal `knowledge-base/` coordinates that do not exist for an npm consumer; behavior unchanged (comment-only removal, `tsc --noEmit` green).


### Fixed
- **Restored four `memories` columns silently dropped by unclosed JSDoc comments in the schema.** Three block comments in `schema.ts` were missing their closing `*/`, so the column definitions right after each (`attributedTo`, `category`, `textLemmatized`, `lastAccessedAt`) were parsed as comment text and never registered on the drizzle model — leaving `table.category` `undefined`, which crashed drizzle `getTableConfig` at the category index and cascaded a compile/runtime failure to every module importing the db barrel (schema tests, eviction, CLI, MCP, server) and broke `db:generate` repo-wide. Closing the three comments restores the columns; typecheck goes from 3 errors to 0 and `db:generate` works again. Pre-existing bug (predates the recall-trace work); regression locked by the existing schema column-list assertion (explainable-recall).

## [0.34.0] - 2026-07-12

### Added
- **Zero-config `createMemory` preset**: `createMemory({ postgres, openai })` boots a working in-process `Memory` from just a Postgres URL + OpenAI key — it wires `OpenAIEmbedder` + `OpenAILLM` + `PgvectorStore` + a `pg.Pool`, auto-runs migrations by default (opt-out `{ migrate: false }`), owns the pool lifecycle (`dispose` also ends the pool), and returns a `DisposableMemory`. Non-breaking: `createMemory` now accepts EITHER the preset shape OR the existing full `LocalMemoryConfig` (discriminated by the `postgres` field); `createLocalMemory` and existing callers are untouched. New public exports `CreateMemoryPresetConfig` (type) + `InvalidPresetConfig` (typed error thrown fail-fast on an empty `postgres`). One OpenAI key per process (the OpenAI client is a module-global lazy singleton); for multiple keys use the full-config path (create-memory-preset).

An explicit caller category always wins; the auto value is trimmed + 1-64-bounded fail-soft; no backfill. Observable via `category_auto_assigned` (auto-categorization-write-pipeline T3.1).

## [0.26.0] - 2026-06-30

### Added
- **Native streamable-HTTP MCP transport now binds the per-request user scope from the
 `X-Theo-User-Id` header** (ADR D3 — the binding deferred at T6.2). Mirrors the SSE
 transport (which binds `userId` from the URL path) so tool calls resolve a scope via
 `memoryFromCtx` instead of failing "Scope not bound". The id is sanitized (EC-16: safe
 charset, bounded length); absent/invalid header → no scope (scope-required tools fail
 closed). Unblocks the integrated MCP-Gateway path (`/mcp/*` → Theo MCP Gateway → themory)
 end-to-end: a gateway-injected per-tenant key + this header = a real scoped `tools/call`.
 The user tier is the only one bound (the only tier `memoryFromCtx` resolves); within a
 workspace the tiers are organizational, the workspace/tenant is the security boundary.
- **README — streamable-HTTP over TLS usage (ADR-0015 #3).** Documents `themory mcp
 --transport streamable-http --host 0.0.0.0 --tls-cert/--tls-key` for non-localhost binds
 (both flags required together; localhost stays plain HTTP), and notes the theo-workspace
 integrated stack runs `themory` from this repo's built `dist/` — so rebuild (`npm run build`)
 after updating to ≥ v0.25.0. (`README.md` § Agent loops via MCP)

### Changed
- **`docker-publish` agora roda no self-hosted runner (minutos GitHub-hosted estão billing-blocked).** O job `build-and-publish` passou de `runs-on: ubuntu-latest` para `runs-on: [self-hosted, linux, x64]` — o runner `theo-ci-theo-memory` no droplet `theo-ci-runner`. Docker + buildx já presentes no host; o Trivy continua vindo da `aquasecurity/trivy-action` (não precisa binário no host). Build + scan + push (GHCR + Docker Hub) da imagem `themory` rodam no droplet. (`.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml`)

### Deprecated

### Removed

### Fixed
- **`docker-publish` referenciava uma versão inexistente do Trivy → "Set up job" falhava sempre.** `aquasecurity/trivy-action@0.24.0` nunca foi uma tag publicada (404 — "unable to find version"); falhava na resolução da action em qualquer runner. Pinado em `v0.36.0` (estável atual; mesmos inputs). (`.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml`)
- **`docker-publish` não parseava → o workflow inteiro não rodava (nem disparava).** Dois steps usavam `if: ${{ secrets.DOCKERHUB_TOKEN != '' }}`, mas o contexto `secrets` **não é permitido em `if:`** — o GitHub rejeitava o workflow com `Unrecognized named-value: 'secrets'` (HTTP 422 no dispatch; push/tag triggers idem). O token agora é elevado a um `env` de nível job (`DOCKERHUB_TOKEN: ${{ secrets.DOCKERHUB_TOKEN }}`) e os guards usam `env.DOCKERHUB_TOKEN`, que é válido em `if:`. (`.github/workflows/docker-publish.yml`)

### Security
- **MCP tool surface now enforces per-tenant workspace isolation — closes a cross-tenant
 data leak (theo-memory#82).** The `themory mcp` server authenticated an API key as a
 boolean and served a single shared, workspace-UNBOUND `Memory` to every request, so any
 valid key could read/write ANY workspace's data (the MCP twin of the gap #77 closed for
 REST). The bearer gate now resolves a `Principal` from the key and binds
 `Memory.withWorkspace(workspaceId)` at every tool memory-access site (the new
 `workspaceScopedMemory` seam), fed by an auth-trusted `ScopedContext.workspaceId` (never a
 client header). Fail-closed: a key resolving no/blank workspace → 401; a Memory that cannot
 bind the workspace → typed error, never unbound. Scoped to the api-keys streamable-HTTP path
 (the gateway-fronted path #82 exploited); OIDC/SSE multi-tenant workspace mapping tracked as
 a follow-up. (#82)
- **Imagem `themory` limpa no gate Trivy (CRITICAL+HIGH) — 11 CVEs de dependência corrigidos, 6 do base image documentados (issue #86).** Via `pnpm.overrides`: `protobufjs` 6.11.6→7.6.1 (corrige a CRITICAL CVE-2026-41242 + 5 HIGH), `form-data`→4.0.6, `linkify-it`→5.0.1, `lodash`→4.18.1, `tmp`→0.2.7, `underscore`→1.13.8. O bump major do `protobufjs` foi validado (build/tsc + roundtrip TensorProto via onnx-proto, o caminho do embedder/reranker local). Os 6 CVEs do OpenSSL/`libssl3` vêm do base distroless `nodejs22-debian12:nonroot` (ainda em 3.0.18; fix publicado no Debian mas não no rebuild do distroless) — não-patcháveis num runtime distroless; allowlistados no `.trivyignore` com sunset 2026-07-30 (CRITICAL é 32-bit-only, não explorável em amd64). Scan local: 0 vulnerabilidades, gate exit 0. (`package.json`, `pnpm-lock.yaml`, `.trivyignore`)

## [0.25.0] - 2026-06-30

### Added
- **Dev TLS for the streamable-http MCP transport (ADR-0015 #3 plumbing).** `themory mcp --transport streamable-http` now accepts `--tls-cert <path>` / `--tls-key <path>`: when both are supplied the listener binds via `https.createServer`, so the MCP server can run on a non-localhost host (e.g. `--host 0.0.0.0`, reached by a containerized client over `host.docker.internal`) while honoring ADR-0015 #3 (HTTPS required for any non-localhost host). Supplying only one of the two flags fails fast (`--tls-cert and --tls-key must be provided together`); supplying neither preserves the unchanged localhost path. The `StreamableHttpOptions.tls` interface that previously only satisfied the guard is now actually wired into the listener. (mcp-gateway-local-compose — theo-traefik-mcp Gateway local-compose enablement)

## [0.24.0] - 2026-06-28

### Added
- **`themory remember --no-infer` verbatim flag (CLI ↔ REST parity).** The CLI `remember` verb now mirrors the REST `POST /v1/remember` `infer: false` option: `--no-infer` stores the text verbatim and skips the two-stage LLM fact-extraction. Previously a CLI write → recall round-trip depended entirely on extraction, which cannot be made deterministic on a no-LLM / mock-LLM backend (the write landed in pg but recall returned nothing, with no way to force a verbatim store). Documented in `themory remember --help`. (usetheodev/theo-memory#79)

## [0.23.0] - 2026-06-28

### Added
- **`memory:platform-admin` scope + scope implication.** A new platform-level scope that is the only scope allowed to mint a `theo_live_*` key bound to an arbitrary target workspace (the seam the TheoCloud gateway uses to provision per-tenant downstream credentials, closing the gateway confused-deputy). `memory:platform-admin` is a strict superset of `memory:admin`, so a platform key reaches the existing `/v1/admin/keys/*` lifecycle routes without a separate admin grant. (model-b-platform-admin-mint T1.1, ADR D1/D2)
- **`POST /v1/admin/keys` accepts an optional target `workspace_id`.** A `memory:platform-admin` caller may mint a key bound to an arbitrary workspace, which is idempotently auto-provisioned (`INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) in the mint path so the TheoCloud gateway can issue one downstream credential per tenant. A non-platform caller supplying a foreign `workspace_id` is rejected `403 INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE_FOR_TARGET`; an out-of-shape id is rejected `400 INVALID_WORKSPACE_ID`; omitting it preserves the existing caller-workspace binding. (model-b-platform-admin-mint T1.2, ADR D3)


### Changed
- **Opt-in fail-closed multi-tenant auth mode (`THEMEMORY_REQUIRE_CREDENTIAL` / `createServer({ requireCredential })`).** When enabled, the bearer middleware refuses the no-credential and verify-only `DEFAULT_PRINCIPAL` collapse (returns `401`), so a multi-tenant deployment cannot silently grant the shared `default` owner workspace on misconfiguration. Defaults to off — single-tenant dev/CI is unchanged. `themory server` resolves the env flag at boot. (model-b-platform-admin-mint T2.1, ADR D4)


### Fixed
- **Standalone host run (`node dist/agent-tools/cli.js server` / `npx themory server`) no longer fails with `ERR_MODULE_NOT_FOUND: Cannot find package 'eld'`.** The flattened `dist/` (which moves `packages/core` to `dist/core`) resolves a workspace runtime dependency (`eld`, from `packages/core`) up the tree to the repo-root `node_modules`; pnpm's default isolated layout left it under `packages/core/node_modules` where the flattened dist could not reach it. Set `node-linker=hoisted` in `.npmrc` so the local install matches what the Dockerfile already uses — the dist now runs standalone on the host without building the image. (theo-workspace local-env live-test enablement, 2026-06-25)


### Security
- **Data-plane scope enforcement on every memory verb (`requireScopeOnPrincipal`).** Each data route now gates on the scope held by the already-resolved bearer Principal: `remember`/`reflect` and the skill write/execute routes require `memory:write`, `recall` and every `GET /v1/scopes/*` read require `memory:read`, and `forget`/scoped-delete require `memory:delete` — a credential missing the verb's scope is rejected `403 INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE`. Previously any authenticated credential could read/write/delete regardless of its granted scopes (a read-only key could write; the gateway's `memory:platform-admin` provisioning key could touch tenant data). Because `memory:platform-admin` implies only `memory:admin`, the platform key is now `403` on every data verb — least privilege (NIST AC-6): it mints per-tenant keys, it never reads or writes tenant data. Closes the data-plane half of the Model B confused-deputy (CWE-441). (usetheodev/theo-memory#77)
- **Mint-scope anti-escalation guard on `POST /v1/admin/keys`.** A caller may no longer mint a key carrying `memory:platform-admin` unless it already holds that scope (`403 INSUFFICIENT_SCOPE_FOR_MINT`). Without this, a `memory:admin` key (which passes the admin route gate) could self-mint `memory:platform-admin` and then target arbitrary workspaces — re-opening the confused-deputy (CWE-441) the slice exists to close. Base scopes (read/write/delete) and `memory:admin` remain freely mintable by an admin (lifecycle unchanged). Key rotation also fails closed on a missing `workspace_id` rather than defaulting to the shared `default` workspace. (model-b-platform-admin-mint /review BLOCKER + MEDIUM)

## [0.22.0] - 2026-06-24

### Added
- **Episodic tier now first-class over MCP — native `episodes` tool.** The cursor-paginated, bi-temporal episodic read surface shipped in v0.14.0 (SDK `ScopedMemory.episodes` + REST `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/episodes`) is now also an MCP tool, so agents/MCP clients (the primary consumer + the `/dogfood` anchor) can list a scope's chronological memory log over MCP. The `episodes` tool resolves scope via the SAME `getScope`+`memoryFromCtx` binding as `recall` (no scope arg — anti-spoof) and delegates to the EXISTING `ScopedMemory.episodes(options)` — no change to the core method, episode types, REST/SDK surface, schema, or migrations; no new dependency. Input: `after`/`before` (opaque cursors, mutually exclusive → -32602), `limit` (≤200), `order` (asc/desc), `queryText` (literal ILIKE), `asOf` (ISO 8601 → bi-temporal replay). Output ships `outputSchema` + `structuredContent` `{ items, nextCursor }` (the LLM-visible MemoryItem subset, like recall). MCP toolset grows 7→8; OpenMemory compat toolset unchanged (native-only — no cursor analogue). (mcp-episodes-tool, G3-MCP)

## [0.21.2] - 2026-06-23

### Changed
- **Migration policy: large-table index builds run `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` out-of-band (documented + guarded).** drizzle-orm 0.45.2 `migrate` runs all pending migrations inside ONE transaction, so `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY` (forbidden in a transaction) can never live in a migration file — it would crash `runMigrations` at deploy (SQLSTATE 25001). New runbook [`architecture.md § 10.4`](.claude/rules/architecture.md) documents the out-of-band-before-deploy procedure for large production tables (build CONCURRENTLY on a direct connection + `IF NOT EXISTS` → the migration's plain `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS` no-ops at deploy), the INVALID-index recovery, and ADR-0018 records the decision + rejected alternatives. A new guard `tests/integration/migration-no-concurrently.test.ts` (path-drift-sentinel + comment-tolerant, whole-file scan) fails if any journal-applied migration `.sql` smuggles `CREATE INDEX CONCURRENTLY`, catching the deploy-crash footgun at test time. No production/migration code change; plain in-transaction `CREATE INDEX` for small migrations is unchanged. (migration-concurrently-index-runbook, M4)

## [0.21.1] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- **Cross-repo skills listing no longer Seq-Scans the shared tenant partition (SEC-1).** `PgvectorStore.listSkillsBySubject`'s tenant-isolating prefix predicate `agent_id LIKE '{subject}:%'` could NOT use a btree index under a non-`C` collation (e.g. `en_US.utf8`): the default `text_ops` opclass degrades `LIKE 'prefix%'` to a post-scan `Filter`, forcing a Seq Scan over the `workspace_id='default'` partition — which, under the gateway master-key model, holds EVERY customer's skills (a perf/DOS concern that grows with total skill volume; deep `offset` compounds it). FIX: a partial composite index `idx_memories_skill_subject_prefix` on `(workspace_id, agent_id text_pattern_ops) WHERE kind='skill' AND t_invalid IS NULL` (migration `0021`, idempotent `CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS`, declared in `schema.ts` for `db:push` parity). The `text_pattern_ops` opclass compares by raw byte order, making the prefix a left-anchored index range (`agent_id ~>=~ 'x' AND agent_id ~<~ 'y'`) regardless of collation. NO query change (the planner selects the index automatically; node-postgres unnamed statements get a per-call custom plan so the bound `'{subject}:%'` is a constant at plan time → the index is used in production), NO public-API change, `listSkillsBySubject` results byte-unchanged. THE GOAL TEST (`tests/integration/skills-subject-prefix-index.test.ts`): against real `ankane/pgvector:v0.5.1` with ~2000 seeded skills, the index ships with the `text_pattern_ops` opclass (structural) AND `EXPLAIN` (under `enable_seqscan=off`, literal prefix) shows a `Bitmap Index Scan on idx_memories_skill_subject_prefix` with an `Index Cond` range — never a `Seq Scan on memories`; the cross-repo GOAL stays 5/5 green. (skills-subject-prefix-index, M2)

## [0.21.0] - 2026-06-23

### Added
- **Cross-repo skills listing (`GET /v1/scopes/skill/:subject/all` + `Memory.listSkillsBySubject`).** A tenant-safe flat listing of ALL skills for a subject across EVERY repo, each row carrying its `repoId` — so a browse surface (e.g. the dashboard Skills tab) can list-always instead of requiring a typed repoId upfront. Tenant isolation is enforced by an `agent_id` prefix scan (`agent_id LIKE '{subject}:%'` with LIKE-wildcards escaped + `ESCAPE '\'`) AND the bound `workspace_id` — a subject containing `%`/`_`/`\`/`:` cannot broaden the match (no cross-tenant leak; subject with `:` → 400). Offset pagination (`limit`/`offset`) + optional `q` description substring filter + `total`/`truncated` disclosure. The shipped per-repo `GET /v1/scopes/skill/:repoId/skills` + `ScopedSkillMemory.getAll` are byte-unchanged (additive); no schema/migration, no new dependency. New `SkillWithRepo`/`SkillsBySubjectOptions`/`SkillsBySubjectPage` exported from `@usetheo/memory/contract`; `Memory` widens by one method (`listSkillsBySubject`). THE GOAL TEST: against real `ankane/pgvector:v0.5.1`, seeding `alice:repo-a` (2) + `alice:repo-b` (1) + `bob:repo-c` (1) → `listSkillsBySubject('alice')` returns exactly the 3 alice skills (correct `repoId`) and NONE of bob's; strict-prefix (`al` vs `alice`) + wildcard-escape isolation proven. (skills-cross-repo-flat-list)

## [0.20.6] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- **Scoped recall no longer starved by a dense shared HNSW index (EG-6).** The COMPLETE live-test found `recall` / `search_memory` intermittently returning 0 results in the running dev stack even though the in-scope memory existed with a healthy embedding. ROOT CAUSE: on pgvector 0.5.1 the HNSW index fetches the global `hnsw.ef_search` (default 40) nearest vectors and applies the `WHERE user_id=…` scope filter AFTER — so a sparse scope whose rows are not among the global nearest is starved to 0 (the well-documented ANN+filter recall degradation; pgvector 0.8.0 iterative scans would fix it natively but the runtime is pinned to `ankane/pgvector:v0.5.1`). This was invisible to the isolated-DB GOAL tests (all rows in-scope → no starvation). FIX: a SELECTIVE (scope-filtered) `PgvectorStore.search` now runs the vector ordering as an EXACT distance sort over the scope-filtered subset — a `MATERIALIZED` CTE filters by the scope btree FIRST, then the outer query sorts that small set by `<=>` exactly — guaranteeing recall@k regardless of shared-index density. Unscoped search (no filter — not produced by `recall` today) keeps the plain HNSW path. NO schema/migration/DIP/public-export change, NO new dependency; returned columns + bi-temporal predicate + param order preserved. New dense-multi-scope GOAL (`tests/integration/recall-scoped-exact-search.test.ts`) proves a sparse target scope recalls its memory against real `ankane/pgvector:v0.5.1` with a deterministic non-vacuous assertion (the target ranks beyond the global ef_search frontier by exact distance, so HNSW-then-filter would return 0). The exact-vs-HNSW branch keys on the presence of any selective WHERE clause (`filter.sql`), not on param count — so a param-less clause (`excludeSuperseded` → `superseded_by IS NULL`) also takes the exact path instead of being silently dropped into the unscoped branch (closes /review ARCH-3). (recall-scoped-exact-search)
- **Lint debt closed (Item 13 adaptive + graph-aware-recall).** Pre-existing repo-wide lint errors committed 2026-06-22 (5 files: `_scope` unused-arg false-positive, `type`-vs-`interface`, import order, unnecessary type assertion) are fixed; `eslint.config.ts` now honors the universal `^_`-prefix convention for intentionally-unused params (`argsIgnorePattern`), eliminating that false-positive class. `npm run lint` is green. (recall-scoped-exact-search)

## [0.20.5] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- **Entity/relation label language determinism (EG-5).** The extracted `entities[]` names + `relations[]` labels now reliably stay in the input language. EG-4 (v0.20.4) pinned the `memory[].text` fact, but the COMPLETE live-test surfaced a residual: an English input still produced Portuguese graph nodes (`surfar`, `fins de semana`) next to English ones — poisoning the dashboard Graph/Entities tabs and splitting entity dedup across languages. The write pipeline now normalizes labels to the input language before persist: when the input language is detected (`eld`) and labels exist, ONE corrective LLM call returns a `{ original: target-language }` map applied to entity names AND relation source/relationship/destination (the SAME surface → the SAME translation, so a relation endpoint stays connected to its renamed node — referential integrity); the literal `user` speaker token is never translated; a new `extraction_label_language_mismatch` OTel event reports `{input_language, label_count, corrected}` (Princípio 3). FAIL-SOFT: the guard NEVER drops a node/edge nor fails `remember`. Hardened real-LLM GOAL (EN labels EN, PT labels PT) stable 4/4 vs real OpenAI + pgvector; live dev-stack re-verify confirms EN entity labels. NO new dependency (reuses `eld`). Trade-off (honest): one extra LLM call per `remember` that extracts entities (a future optimization can merge it with the EG-4 fact-correction call). (entity-label-language-determinism)

## [0.20.4] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- **Extraction language determinism (EG-4 fixes-cycle-1).** The persisted `memory[].text` fact now reliably stays in the input language. The shipped v0.20.3 prompt-only fix was proven insufficient by the integrated live-test: with the real `gpt-4o-mini`, English inputs (e.g. "I work as a data scientist and I love rock climbing") were still stored as Portuguese facts ("cientista de dados", "escalada"). The write pipeline now (1) deterministically detects the input language (`eld`, Apache-2.0, pure-JS), (2) injects the EXPLICIT target-language name into the extraction prompt (a concrete instruction the model follows, vs the unreliable self-detection), and (3) runs a fail-soft post-extraction guard that focus-rewrites any residual mismatched fact into the input language and emits the `extraction_language_mismatch` OTel event (Princípio 3). The guard NEVER drops a fact nor fails `remember`. Hardened real-LLM GOAL (non-cognate EN/PT) passes against real OpenAI + pgvector. New dependency: `eld@^1.0.1`. (extraction-language-determinism-fixes-cycle-1)

## [0.20.3] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- Extraction language determinism (EG-4): the write-pipeline LLM now keeps the persisted `memory[].text` fact in the SAME language as the input instead of translating it non-deterministically (an English input was sometimes stored as a Portuguese fact, e.g. "i live in Lisbon" → "user mora em Lisboa"). The extraction prompt's source-language rule — which already pinned entity names + relation labels — was extended to cover the fact text too (preserve source language, never translate; the literal "user" speaker token remains the only exception). Consistent fact language keeps dedup stable (the same fact hashes equally) and the Memories tab predictable. Verified with a real-OpenAI GOAL (PT input → PT fact, EN input → EN fact). `packages/core` prompt clause only; ZERO new dependency, no migration, no DIP widening, no public-export change. Surfaced by the 2026-06-23 Chrome-MCP end-user dogfood. (extraction-language-determinism)

## [0.20.2] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- Distinct-entities metric consistency (EG-3): the `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/metrics` `entity_count` (shown as "Distinct entities" on the dashboard Overview) now counts DISTINCT graph entities from the same source the Entities/Graph tab reads (`MemoryEntityStore.list`) when the entity read port is wired — instead of distinct `attributedTo` peers, which made the Overview report "1" while the Entities tab showed "10" for the same scope. Single source of truth: the two can no longer diverge. Honest `attributedTo` fallback retained when no entity store is wired (in-memory adapter / tests), and the entities handler uses the identical fallback so the two stay consistent on that path too. `packages/api` only; ZERO new dependency, no DIP widening, no migration, no public-export change. Surfaced by the 2026-06-23 Chrome-MCP end-user dogfood. (distinct-entities-metric-fix)

## [0.20.1] - 2026-06-23

### Fixed
- Entity-graph noise filter (EG-2): the entity-graph no longer stores pronoun/contraction/generic-role/verb-fragment nodes (`I'm`, `m reading`, `love science`, `user`) that polluted the Entities tab + Graph view. A new pure deterministic `isEntityNoise` predicate is applied at the `linkEntities` chokepoint, so it cleans BOTH the LLM-primary AND the compromise-fallback node sources (the LLM path previously had no content filter), plus defense-in-depth in the compromise extractor. Precision-biased + recall-safe: drops only KNOWN English noise + structural fragments; multilingual proper nouns (`São Paulo`, `José`), capitalized-proper compounds (`Love Island`) and verb-homograph noun compounds (`watch list`, `think tank`) are preserved. Fail-soft (a pure total function inside the Phase-7 try/catch; never fails `remember`). ZERO new dependency, no DIP widening, no public export, no migration. Surfaced by the 2026-06-23 Chrome-MCP end-user dogfood of v0.20.0. (entity-extraction-noise-filter)

## [0.20.0] - 2026-06-23

### Added
- Graph-aware recall over REST: `POST /v1/recall` now accepts an optional `graphExpand` field (`{ hops?: 1 | 2; limit?: number }`), exposing the entity-graph-augmented retrieval (shipped v0.19.0 at the SDK layer) to REST / gateway / MCP / HTTP consumers. Mirrors `RecallOptions.graphExpand`; bounded by a strict zod schema (`hops` ∈ {1,2}, `limit` ≤ 100 — DoS-guard parity with `topK`); the OpenAPI 3.1 spec auto-derives the field. DEFAULT OFF — a recall without `graphExpand` is byte-identical to before; an invalid `graphExpand` is rejected `422` (never `500`). Additive, non-breaking, ZERO new dependency, NO core change. (rest-graph-aware-recall)

## [0.19.0] - 2026-06-23

### Added
- Graph-aware recall — entity-graph-augmented retrieval (evolves the entity-relation-extraction feature; project-owner direction 2026-06-22 to evolve the existing Postgres entity-graph, NOT a graph-DB backend): a new opt-in `GraphExpansionStore` DIP (`expandByEntities`) + a recall pipeline Step 8.7 that POOL-EXPANDS results with memories CONNECTED to the top results by a shared entity (1 hop) or relation edge (opt-in 2nd hop) via JOINs over the EXISTING `entities`/`memory_entities`/`entity_edges` tables — surfacing connected memories that did not pass the similarity threshold on their own. Enable per-call via `RecallOptions.graphExpand` ({ hops?: 1|2; limit? }); the default `PgGraphExpansionStore` (the JOIN) is auto-wired by the factory (NO new dependency, NO migration, NO poly-store). Graph hits are scored BELOW genuine semantic hits (never outrank a real match); seeds are deduped (EC-1); candidates are scope-confined (EC-2 — no cross-scope leak); fail-soft (a traversal outage degrades to the non-graph pool, never crashes recall) + 2 observable events (`recall_graph_expanded`/`recall_graph_failed`, Princípio 3). A wired+requested graph-expand bypasses the recall cache. DEFAULT OFF — byte-identical when omitted. (graph-aware-recall)

## [0.18.0] - 2026-06-22

### Added
- Adaptive retrieval — Self-RAG / CRAG decision hooks (Roadmap Item 13): a new opt-in `AdaptiveRetrievalPolicy` DIP (`shouldRetrieve` pre-embed gate + `assess` post-threshold grade-and-correct) wired into `recall`, DEFAULT OFF (byte-unchanged). When a policy is wired via `createLocalMemory({ retrievalPolicy })`, a `shouldRetrieve` returning `{retrieve:false}` makes `recall` return `[]` WITHOUT embedding the query (Self-RAG skip — saves embed + DB cost); a CRAG `assess` recommending `relax` re-filters the already-fetched pool at a lower threshold (bounded in-memory corrective — NO re-embed, NO external fallback). Default impl `HeuristicAdaptiveRetrievalPolicy` (key-free, no new dependency) ships behind `@usetheo/memory/adaptive-local` (bundle-isolated). Tune per-call via `RecallOptions.adaptive`. Fail-soft (a policy outage degrades to normal retrieval, never crashes recall) + 4 observable events (`retrieval_skipped` / `retrieval_corrected` / `retrieval_low_confidence` / `retrieval_policy_failed`, Princípio 3 — a policy crash emits the distinct `retrieval_policy_failed`, never silent). EC-1 clamp: a relax applies only when `0 <= relaxedThreshold < threshold`. A wired policy bypasses the semantic recall cache (the cache key does not encode the adaptive outcome). (adaptive-retrieval; Item 13)

## [0.17.0] - 2026-06-22

### Added
- Embedder registry + 3 new fetch-based providers (Roadmap Item 24): `createEmbedder(spec)` — a string-keyed factory that resolves an `Embedder` from a provider key (`stub`/`openai`/`local`/`ollama`/`voyage`/`google`) via LAZY dynamic import, so the `@usetheo/memory/embedders/registry` subpath's static graph stays free of the heavy SDK deps (`openai`, `@xenova/transformers`) — bundle isolation. Three NEW first-party adapters with ZERO new dependency (Node-global `fetch`, injectable for testing): `OllamaEmbedder` (`@usetheo/memory/embedders/ollama`), `VoyageEmbedder` (`@usetheo/memory/embedders/voyage`), `GoogleEmbedder` (Gemini, `@usetheo/memory/embedders/google`). Unknown provider key → `EmbedderError` listing the valid keys (fail-loud); an SDK-backed provider whose dependency is not installed → the new `EmbedderUnavailableError` with an install hint. Narrows the 3-vs-8 provider gap surfaced by the the reference project cross-validation. (embedder-registry; Item 24)

## [0.16.0] - 2026-06-22

### Added
- Optional domain ontologies (Roadmap Item 15): a typed per-domain `Ontology` vocabulary (entity types + relation types; built-ins `medical`/`legal`/`code`/`support`) layered over the LLM extraction pipeline. Enable via `createLocalMemory({ ontology: 'medical' })` (registry key) or a custom `Ontology` object. The ontology vocabulary is injected into the extraction prompt (steering the LLM) and validated post-parse: out-of-vocabulary entity types coerce to `OTHER` and emit an observable `ontology_type_coerced` event (Princípio 3); out-of-vocab relations are kept. Persisted in the EXISTING `entities.type` + `entity_edges.relation` columns — NO new store, NO migration (the "ontologies without poly-store" attack on ). Fail-soft: an ontology mismatch never fails `remember`. Default (no ontology) is byte-unchanged. (domain-ontologies; Item 15)

## [0.15.0] - 2026-06-22

### Added
- Optional cross-encoder reranker (Roadmap Item 23): a new `Reranker` DIP + a default `CrossEncoderReranker` (cross-encoder via `@xenova/transformers`, no new dependency) wired as an opt-in post-threshold recall stage. Enable per-call via `recall({ rerank: {} })` with a reranker wired through `createLocalMemory({ reranker })`; the default impl ships behind the `@usetheo/memory/reranker-local` subpath (bundle-isolated). Mutually exclusive with `diversify` (throws `RerankerConflict`); fail-soft to cosine order on inference failure (emits `recall_rerank_failed`); bypasses the semantic recall cache. Default (no `rerank` option) is byte-unchanged. (cross-encoder-reranker; Item 23)
- Opt-in skill execution in the production runtime: `THEOMEM_SKILL_EXECUTION=true` wires `SandboxedSkillExecutor` into the shared `wireMemoryFromPool` factory (so `themory server` + MCP + CLI all honor one flag). Default OFF — the secure STORE-only default + the `501 SKILL_EXECUTION_NOT_CONFIGURED` response are unchanged when unset. (production-runtime-hardening; surfaced by the M2 live-test)


### Fixed
- M1 skill execution `503 SKILL_EXECUTOR_UNAVAILABLE` in the distroless runtime: the `production-deps` stage used pnpm's default isolated node_modules, so the OPTIONAL native deps (`isolated-vm`, `onnxruntime-node`) never reached the top-level node_modules the distroless runtime copies → unreachable from the flattened `/app/dist`. Fixed by installing production-deps with `--config.node-linker=hoisted` (flat layout); verified a skill executes end-to-end in the distroless image (HTTP 200). Also fixes the local embedder (`onnxruntime-node`) in the published image. (production-runtime-hardening)
- `isolated-vm` pinned `^7.0.0` → `^6.1.2` (M1 dependency-compatibility bug): `isolated-vm@7` declares `engines: node>=26`, but the project's runtime is **Node 22** (Docker base + `distroless/nodejs22`). pnpm correctly OMITS the optional dep on Node 22 (unmet engines), so the image could not build from HEAD AND M1 skill-execution could never run on the Node 22 distroless runtime. `isolated-vm@6.1.2` declares `node>=22` (compatible) and is API-identical for the surface M1 uses (`Isolate`/`createContext`/`compileScript`/`run`/`ExternalCopy`/`dispose`); M1 GOAL test 8/8 + M2 9/9 green on it. (surfaced by the M2 Theo-Cloud live-test)
- Docker image build from HEAD broke since M1 (v0.13.0): the `builder` + `production-deps` stages (slim base) lacked node-gyp's toolchain for the native `isolated-vm`/`onnxruntime-node` builds. Added `python3`/`make`/`g++` to both stages so the `.node` binaries compile and flow to the distroless runtime. (surfaced by the M2 Theo-Cloud live-test)

## [0.14.0] - 2026-06-22

### Added
- First-class episodic / message-log read surface (M2): `ScopedMemory.episodes(opts?)` returns a cursor-paginated, bi-temporal-aware temporal log of a scope's memories (after/before/limit/order/asOf/queryText). Built over the existing memory substrate — no new storage engine, no new dependency, no new table (cursor `(created_at, id)`). New `VectorStore.listEpisodes` DIP method (pgvector + in-memory). (#M2 first-class-episodes)

## [0.13.0] - 2026-06-22

### Added
- **`SkillExecutor` DIP port** (`@usetheo/memory`) — the contract for executing a stored skill's code-as-skill payload inside a hardened sandbox (`execute(skill, args, opts?) -> SkillExecutionResult`). Pure domain contract (no I/O, no native addon); default impl `SandboxedSkillExecutor` (isolated-vm) lands next. Part of M1 procedural skill execution (gap G2 / Item 21). STORE-only stays the default; execution is opt-in/gated. (#agentic-skill-execution-sandbox T1.1)
- **Skill-execution typed errors + audit events** (`@usetheo/memory`) — `ToolInvocationDeniedError` (with a `ToolInvocationDeniedReason` discriminant over 9 guard literals → REST 403), `SkillExecutionNotConfiguredError` (→ 501), `SkillExecutorUnavailableError` (sandbox backend not installed → 503), plus the `tool_invocation_denied` + `skill_executed` OTel events. Every execution denial is observable (Princípio 3). (#agentic-skill-execution-sandbox T1.2)
- **`execute_skill` MCP tool** (`@usetheo/memory-mcp`) — opt-in tool to run a stored skill over MCP (`{repoId, skillId, args?, opts?}`). Returns the captured `{output,truncated,elapsedMs}`; a guard denial / not-found / not-configured / addon-unavailable maps to a Tool Execution Error with a self-correcting message. The native `tools/list` grows 6 → 7. (#agentic-skill-execution-sandbox T2.4)
- **`POST /v1/scopes/skill/:repoId/skills/:skillId/execute`** (`@usetheo/memory/server`) — REST surface for gated skill execution. Body `{ args, opts? }`. 200 `{output,truncated,elapsedMs}` / 403 guard-denied (with `reason`) / 404 absent-or-cross-scope (no existence leak) / 501 no-executor-wired / 503 sandbox-addon-not-installed / 422 invalid body. OpenAPI-registered (`operationId: executeSkill`). (#agentic-skill-execution-sandbox T2.3)
- **`ScopedSkillMemory.execute(skillId, args, opts?)`** (`@usetheo/memory`) — opt-in/gated SDK entry to run a stored skill: resolves the workspace+scope-isolated skill by id (cross-scope / cross-workspace / absent → `MemoryNotFound` 404, no existence leak) and delegates to the wired `SkillExecutor`. When no executor is wired it throws `SkillExecutionNotConfiguredError` — STORE-only stays the default (the 5 existing skill verbs are unchanged). Enable it with `createLocalMemory({ skillExecutor: new SandboxedSkillExecutor })`. (#agentic-skill-execution-sandbox T2.1-T2.2)
- **`SandboxedSkillExecutor`** (`@usetheo/memory`) — default `SkillExecutor` impl that runs a stored TS/JS skill inside an isolated V8 context (the optional, lazy-loaded `isolated-vm` native addon — NOT pulled by the default barrel). Hardens execution with a 6-guard set + per-call seam: no Node/`fs` bindings (isolate is bindingless by construction, plus a source pre-scan that audits any attempt), structured-arg injection (never string-concatenated into source), wall-clock timeout, output-byte cap, memory cap, and an arg-key identifier guard; async + TypeScript skills are denied in v1. Opt-in (wired via `createLocalMemory({ skillExecutor })` next); STORE-only stays the default. (#agentic-skill-execution-sandbox T1.3)


### Changed
- **PRIORITY 0 feature freeze LIFTED in full** (project-owner sign-off 2026-06-21). Items 13/14/15/21/23/24/25 + Phase V are no longer FROZEN; new features may run the normal cycle. The P0 HARDENING bar (APIs correct / SDKs functional / MCP available) and the SEPARATE `production-ready`/v1.0 gate (`/dogfood` + benchmark, Item 11) are UNCHANGED — the unfreeze is feature scope only, not the honesty gate. Governance only; no consumer code change. (CLAUDE.md § PRIORITY 0 Amendment 2026-06-21; roadmap at `.claude/knowledge-base/roadmaps/memory-gaps-roadmap.md`)

## [0.12.0] - 2026-06-21

### Added
- **Subject-scoped forget** — `ScopedMemory.forget(memoryId, { hard? })` deletes a memory ONLY if it belongs to that scope (+ workspace); a cross-scope / cross-workspace / missing id throws `MemoryNotFound` (→ 404, no existence leak). Backed by a new single-SQL `VectorStore.deleteInScope(id, filters, hard)` (workspace + tier WHERE; soft guards `t_invalid IS NULL` for idempotent-404; hard cascades via the entity-link FKs). Lets a multi-tenant gateway delete-by-id without an O(scope) ownership pre-check. The existing top-level `Memory.forget` is unchanged. (#themory-scoped-forget-and-retrieval-counter T1.1)
- **`DELETE /v1/scopes/:type/:id/memories/:memId`** (`@usetheo/memory/server`) — REST surface for subject-scoped forget. `?hard=true` for GDPR erasure (default soft). 204 (deleted) / 404 (cross-scope / cross-workspace / missing — no existence leak) / 422 (memId not a UUID). Write rate-limited; OpenAPI-registered. (#themory-scoped-forget-and-retrieval-counter T1.2)
- **Real per-scope `retrieval_api_usage`** — `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/metrics` now reports the cumulative number of `recall` calls for the scope instead of a hardcoded 0. Each `recall` increments a persisted per-scope counter (new `scope_usage_counters` table, migration 0020) via the new `UsageCounterStore` DIP + default `PgUsageCounterStore` (atomic `ON CONFLICT` UPSERT). The increment is fail-soft — a counter failure never fails a `recall`. The metrics response keeps its exact 4-field shape; `retrieval_api_usage` falls back to 0 when no counter store is wired (in-memory adapter). Lets a gateway/dashboard read real recall usage without deriving it. (#themory-scoped-forget-and-retrieval-counter T2.1-T2.4)

## [0.11.0] - 2026-06-20

### Added
- Edge port production wiring — `createLocalMemory` wires `PgMemoryGraphStore` by default (same `entityBoost`/PgvectorStore gate as the node port) and `themory server` injects it into `createServer`, so a default `remember` of a relation populates `entity_edges` and `GET /graph` serves real edges in production. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 T3.2)
- **`GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/graph`** (`@usetheo/memory/server`) — returns `{nodes, edges}` for a subject scope (nodes via the entities read port, edges via the new graph port). D6: `edges:[]` when the graph store is not wired. OpenAPI-registered. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 T3.1)
- Relation (edge) extraction wired into Phase 7 — the existing write-pipeline LLM call now also emits optional `relations` triples; `linkEdges` (in the new `entity-linking.ts`) resolves endpoints + persists edges (fail-soft, independent try/catch from node linking; EC-3 cap 50, EC-4 relation length ≤64). `remember` of a relation now populates `entity_edges`. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 T2.2)
- `PgMemoryGraphStore` (default `MemoryGraphStore` impl) — `upsertEdges` resolves endpoint names to entity ids by `(scope, normalized_key)` deterministically (earliest node wins, EC-2) and UPSERTs edges in one round-trip; `listEdges` aggregates `memoryCount = count(DISTINCT source_memory_id)` per triple (EC-1). Exported from `@usetheo/memory`. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 T2.1)
- `MemoryGraphStore` DIP (2 methods: `upsertEdges`/`listEdges`) + `EdgeToLink`/`EdgeSummary` DTOs (exported from `@usetheo/memory/contract`); 2 new `MEMORY_EVENTS` (`entity_edge_linked`, `entity_edge_linking_failed`, fail-soft). (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 T2.0)
- `entity_edges` table (migration 0019, additive) — stores relation triples `{source, relation, destination}` between entity nodes with `source_memory_id` provenance; per-scope UNIQUE includes `source_memory_id` so `memory_count` aggregates across memories. NO graph DB (two relational tables + JOINs). (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 T1.1)


### Changed
- **Entity nodes are now extracted by the LLM (multilingual), not `compromise`.** The write-pipeline LLM call additionally emits an `entities` array (surface names in the input language/script, diacritics preserved); Phase 7 uses these as the PRIMARY node source. `compromise` is demoted to an English-only FALLBACK used only when the LLM returns no entities (e.g. the `infer:false` fast-path). Matches how every SOTA KG system (, GraphRAG, LightRAG, ) extracts nodes — the prompt carries an explicit "keep proper nouns in the original language, never translate/transliterate" instruction. Fixes the entity graph breaking on non-English (e.g. Portuguese) input. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 multilingual)
- Extraction prompt relations guidance broadened (employment/location/usage/composition/membership/education/ownership/reporting) to raise edge recall so more real entities connect. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 multilingual)
- Extraction prompt now (a) represents the first-person speaker as ONE consistent entity named `user` (no more `user`/`Usuário`/`eu` fragmentation of the speaker node) and (b) emits `relationship` labels in the INPUT language too (`trabalha em`, not `works at`). Empirically validated against `gpt-4o-mini` on a pt-BR conversation. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 prompt-refinements)


### Fixed
- `CompromiseEntityExtractor` no longer corrupts accented proper nouns — the ASCII-only `[A-Za-z]` regexes that stripped diacritics ("São Paulo" → "So") are now Unicode-aware (`\p{L}`/`\p{Lu}` with the `u` flag). Correctness fix on the English/offline fallback path. (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 multilingual)
- `CompromiseEntityExtractor` drops predicate/clause-fragment noise that leaked as graph nodes ("is a", "on the", "project uses", "s skip") by trimming function-word edges and rejecting conjugated-verb fragments, while preserving legitimate noun phrases that carry a leading article ("the machine learning engineer" → "machine learning engineer"). (#entity-relation-extraction-slice2 noise-filter)

## [0.10.1] - 2026-06-20

### Changed
- Entity linking (Phase 7) now runs on the `infer:false` verbatim write path too, via a shared `linkEntities` helper — `remember(text, { infer: false })` now extracts + surfaces entities, closing the v0.10.0 caveat (entity linking previously ran on the `infer:true` path only). Still fail-soft + `entityBoost`-gated. (#entity-extraction-nodes-slice1 fixes-cycle, item 2)


### Fixed
- Entity upsert (`PgMemoryEntityStore.upsertAndLink`) now resolves the entity id in ONE round-trip on dedup-collision via `ON CONFLICT … DO UPDATE SET name = entities.name RETURNING id` (a plain `DO NOTHING` returns no row on conflict, forcing a second `SELECT`). First-write-wins on the entity name/embedding is preserved. Perf-only; behavior unchanged. (#entity-extraction-nodes-slice1 fixes-cycle, item 3 / F-L7)
- Entity extraction no longer truncates a sentence-initial proper noun — "John Smith works at Google" now keeps "John Smith" (was dropping the leading capital), and a lone sentence-initial proper noun ("Google is a search engine") is kept, while lone sentence-initial stopwords ("Today", "I", "The") are still dropped for precision. (#entity-extraction-nodes-slice1 fixes-cycle, item 4)

## [0.10.0] - 2026-06-20

### Added
- Entity extraction (nodes) — the `entities` table is now populated from memory text on `remember`. A new `EntityExtractor` DIP (`CompromiseEntityExtractor` default: JS-native NLP via the optional `compromise` package + regex fallback, no Python NER toolkit) extracts PROPER/QUOTED/COMPOUND/NOUN nodes; a new `MemoryEntityStore` DIP (`PgMemoryEntityStore` default) persists them (deduped per `(workspace, scope, type, normalized_key)`) + links them to source memories (`memory_entities` table, migration `0018`). `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/entities` now serves the REAL deduplicated entities with `memory_count` + `last_seen_at` (response shape unchanged) when the read port is wired; the `attributed_to`-derived fallback is preserved otherwise. Entity linking is `entityBoost`-gated (default on) and fail-soft (a failure emits `entity_linking_failed` and never fails `remember`). Caveat: entity linking runs on the `infer:true` write path only; the `infer:false` verbatim fast-path does not yet extract entities (follow-up). (#entity-extraction-nodes-slice1)

## [0.9.0] - 2026-06-19

### Added
- **`POST /v1/scopes/skill/:repoId/skills`** (`@usetheo/memory/server`) — create a skill (store-only; code is never executed). 201 `{id,hash}`; **409 SKILL_ALREADY_EXISTS** on dedup-collision (D1); 422 invalid body/language/empty; 413 oversized code (64 KiB cap); 400 empty repoId/malformed. Workspace-bound. OpenAPI-registered. (skills-write-surface T2.1)
- **`DELETE /v1/scopes/skill/:repoId/skills/:skillId`** — forget (curate) a skill; 204 success, 404 when not a skill in this repo scope (scope-isolated). OpenAPI-registered. (skills-write-surface T2.2, D4)
- MCP tools **`add_skill`** + **`forget_skill`** (`@usetheo/memory-mcp`) — author + curate skills over MCP (the agent flow). Static tool list grows from 4 to 6 (`listChanged:false` preserved). `add_skill` returns a Tool Execution Error on dedup-collision; `forget_skill` is idempotent. Store-only. (skills-write-surface T2.3, D3)
- `SkillAlreadyExists` domain error: `ScopedSkillMemory.add` now maps a dedup-collision (same description → `UNIQUE(hash)`) to this typed error instead of a raw Postgres unique-violation, so the REST/MCP boundary can return 409 (skills-write-surface T1.1, D1). EC-W1: guarded on the hash constraint to never mis-map a future unique index. Exported from `@usetheo/memory/contract`. (#skills-write-surface)


### Changed
- PRIORITY 0 scope amendment (project-owner / CTO `/goal` 2026-06-19): the **skills WRITE surface** (REST `POST /v1/scopes/skill/:repoId/skills` + MCP `add_skill` + forget curation) is re-classified as in-scope P0 hardening — surface exposure of the already-shipped `ScopedSkillMemory.add`/`forget` SDK capability over REST/MCP/gateway so an agent can author its own skills ( flow). STORE-only (no executor/sandbox/code-editor). Items 13/14/15 stay FROZEN. See CLAUDE.md § "Amendment 2026-06-19 (II)". (#skills-write-surface)

## [0.8.0] - 2026-06-19

### Added
- `ScopedSkillMemory.getAll(options?)` — read-only enumeration of a repo's skills (head-of-version-chain by default; `includeSuperseded` to include superseded ancestors; soft-deleted excluded; `[]` for an empty repo). Powers the read-only skills browse surface; distinct from `recall` semantic search. Reuses the `VectorStore.list` primitive (no new SQL). Pre-release additive change to the `ScopedSkillMemory` DIP contract. (plan `dashboard-phase2b-skills-browse` T1.1)
- **`GET /v1/scopes/skill/:repoId/skills`** (`@usetheo/memory/server`) — read-only Repo-tier skills listing returning `{ skills: [{ id, hash, description, code, codeLanguage, createdAt, derivedFrom, supersededBy }], truncated }`. Dedicated sub-resource (the generic `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/memories` keeps returning `501 SKILL_LISTING_NOT_IMPLEMENTED` for `type=skill`, ADR D5). Resolves skills via the workspace-bound Memory (workspace-isolation parity); `truncated:true` discloses the `topK` cap honestly. OpenAPI registered. (plan `dashboard-phase2b-skills-browse` T2.1)


### Changed
- PRIORITY 0 scope amendment (project-owner / CTO sign-off 2026-06-19): the read-only **Skills / Repo-tier dashboard browse surface** (a new skills listing REST endpoint + gateway BFF + dashboard Skills tab) is UNFROZEN and re-classified as in-scope P0 hardening. It exposes a listing of the already-shipped `Memory.skills(repoId)` SDK capability; read-only browse only (no executor/editor/sandbox). Items 13/14/15 stay FROZEN. See CLAUDE.md § "Amendment 2026-06-19". (#dashboard-phase2b-skills-browse)


### Fixed
- Docker image build is now reproducible: pinned `sharp` to `^0.33.5` via a pnpm override (npm-hosted libvips, no GitHub-CDN download), so `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` in the image build no longer fails when the flaky GitHub CDN times out fetching `sharp@0.32.6`'s libvips. `sharp` is pulled transitively by `@xenova/transformers` (the local embedder) and runs its postinstall under the pnpm build-allowlist; the text-embedding path never invokes `sharp` at runtime, and `@xenova/transformers` imports cleanly against 0.33.x. Makes `make e2e` (which builds the image from source) deterministic again (#memory-integration).

## [0.7.0] - 2026-06-19

### Added
- **Subject-scoped `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/metrics` + `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/entities`** (plan `theo-memory-dashboard-full-integration` T2.1/T2.2, ADR D2 amendment). `metrics` returns real `{ total_memories, entity_count, add_events, retrieval_api_usage }` (counts from the scope's active rows; `entity_count` = distinct `attributed_to`; `retrieval_api_usage` honest-0 — no persisted recall counter yet). `entities` aggregates the distinct `attributed_to` peers (v1.1 multi-peer attribution) with `memory_count` + `last_seen_at`. **Scope = SUBJECT, not workspace** — the theo-cloud gateway shares one master key across customers and isolates by subject-in-path, so a workspace aggregate would leak cross-customer totals. No new table/migration/pipeline (the standalone entity-boost table is unpopulated; richer NER extraction is a follow-up). OpenAPI registered. P0 hardening. 5 new tests (#memory-integration).
- `GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/memories` now accepts optional `from`/`to` RFC3339 query params filtering by `created_at` (inclusive bounds), so the theo-cloud dashboard's Memories date-range inputs become a real server-side filter instead of a no-op control (plan `theo-memory-dashboard-full-integration` ADR D3 / gap 7). Invalid datetimes are rejected at the boundary (422 `INVALID_QUERY`). OpenAPI registration extended with `from`/`to` (and the previously-undocumented `category`) query params. P0 hardening — honest behavior of an already-shipped surface (Inquebrável Rule 3) (#memory-integration).


### Fixed
- **`GET /v1/scopes/:type/:id/{metrics,entities}` now surface real `attributed_to` aggregation.** The handlers read the attributed peer from `metadata.attributed_to` (where `ScopedMemory.getAll` projects it) with the top-level `attributedTo` field as a fallback — previously they read only the top-level field, which `getAll` does NOT populate, so `entity_count`/`entities` were always 0/empty against real pgvector (latent bug surfaced by the theo-cloud Phase 2A attribution round-trip e2e). Unit fixtures updated to the real getAll contract (attributed_to in metadata) (#memory-integration).

## [0.6.0] - 2026-06-18

### Added
- Runtime embedder is now selectable via `THEOMEM_EMBEDDER`: `local` (default — Xenova MiniLM, 384-dim, no API key, back-compat) or `openai` (`text-embedding-3-small`, 1536-dim; requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`). The `openai` path needs NO `@xenova/transformers`/`onnxruntime-node`/`sharp` at runtime and performs NO HuggingFace model download on cold start. Migration dimension follows the selected embedder; unknown values fail loud (no silent fallback).


### Fixed
- `Memory.reset(scope)` (administrative/GDPR full-scope wipe) again physically removes ALREADY-forgotten (soft-deleted) rows. The list soft-delete fix had regressed it (reset enumerates via `list`, now live-only); `list` gained an opt-in `includeSoftDeleted` param that `reset` uses to enumerate tombstones. (/review post-v0.5.0 F-dom-mem-1)
- Destructive embedding-dimension change (triggered by switching `THEOMEM_EMBEDDER` on an existing deployment) now FAILS CLOSED on a populated database: it refuses to wipe unless `THEMEMORY_ALLOW_DESTRUCTIVE_DIM_CHANGE=1` is set. A fresh (0-row) database still migrates silently. Prevents a routine env edit from silently destroying all memories. (/review post-v0.5.0 F-dom-cli-1)
- `themory migrate` is now embedder-aware — it provisions the schema at the dimension matching `THEOMEM_EMBEDDER` (local 384 / openai 1536), so a standalone migrate matches what server/mcp boot would create. Previously it left the schema at the shipped `vector(1536)` default. (/review post-v0.5.0 F-wire-1)
- `getAll`/`list` (scopes endpoint `GET /v1/scopes/.../memories`, OpenMemory `list_memories`, dashboard listing, reflection Orient, eviction candidate scan) now EXCLUDES soft-deleted memories (`t_invalid IS NOT NULL`), matching `recall`/`search`. Previously a memory you `forget`/`delete_memories` still reappeared in listings while being correctly hidden from recall. (`PgvectorStore.list`)
- Clean-checkout build restored: `build:core` now runs BEFORE the main `tsc` pass so cross-package `@usetheo/memory/*` subpath imports resolve against core's freshly-built `.d.ts`. Previously a build from a state without `packages/core/dist` (clean clone / CI / Docker) failed with `TS2307 Cannot find module '@usetheo/memory/theokit'`; it only "worked" incrementally.
- Docker image build restored after the npm→pnpm migration + monorepo split (was broken since 2026-06-15; published `0.1.0-alpha` predates it): `.dockerignore` no longer excludes `pnpm-lock.yaml` (the builder runs `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile`) nor `scripts/` (postbuild:rewrite-aliases needs it); `Dockerfile` copies ALL workspace packages (`core/api/mcp/agent-core/cli`), not just `packages/cli`.
- Docker runtime embedder fixed: `INCLUDE_OPTIONAL` now defaults to `true` so `onnxruntime-node` (the default LocalTransformerEmbedder's ONNX backend — an OPTIONAL dependency of `@xenova/transformers`) ships in the runtime image. Previously `--no-optional` stripped it and the embedder crashed at runtime (`Cannot find package 'onnxruntime-node'`). Set `TRANSFORMERS_CACHE=/tmp/.transformers-cache` so the distroless nonroot image can cache the model (was EACCES-ing).

## [0.5.0] - 2026-06-18

### Added
- OpenMemory MCP-shape compatibility — CLI wiring (openmemory-mcp-compat T2.1): `themory mcp --compat openmemory` (or env `THEOMEM_MCP_COMPAT=openmemory`) boots the OpenMemory tool surface over SSE on port 8765 (default) — the runnable drop-in for OpenMemory-wired MCP clients. Native `themory mcp` is unchanged.
- OpenMemory MCP-shape compatibility — SSE transport + GOAL TEST (openmemory-mcp-compat T1.1/T3.1): `connectSse` mounts the OpenMemory connection convention (`GET /{client}/sse/{user}` + POST message routes + `/{client}/http/{user}` Streamable HTTP) using the SDK's `SSEServerTransport` (no new dep); `user_id` is parsed from the URL path and bound per message via `withScope` (local-first scoping, blueprint D3). Proven by `tests/integration/openmemory/mcp-compat.test.ts` — a real `@modelcontextprotocol/sdk` client over SSE against real pgvector: tools/list = the 4 names, add→search→delete round-trip, and per-user isolation.
- OpenMemory MCP-shape compatibility — Phase 0 (openmemory-mcp-compat T0.1): an opt-in `toolset: 'openmemory'` on the MCP server exposes the OpenMemory tool surface (`add_memories`/`search_memory`/`list_memories`/`delete_memories`) as an alias over the native `remember`/`recall`/`getAll`/`forget` verbs, with OpenMemory-parity JSON-string returns and `delete_memories(memory_ids)` mapping to forget-per-id. Adapter-only (`packages/mcp`); `src/core` untouched. Roadmap Item 26 (P0 sub-goal 3 — MCP), NOT the cut data-adapter (no `the vendor SDK` dep).


### Security

- OpenMemory MCP compat (openmemory-mcp-compat /review): `delete_memories` now enforces per-user ownership before deleting — on the multi-user SSE path a user can no longer delete another user's memory by id (cross-user IDOR closed). add/search/list were already user-scoped; delete now matches. Regression covered by a unit guard + an end-to-end IDOR test against real pgvector.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-06-18

### Added

- Workspace isolation completed for the secondary stores — Slice 1 is now 100% (multi-tenant-rbac-and-slice1-completion T3.1): the opt-in recall-cache, skill store, and eviction pipeline now thread `workspace_id` end-to-end. The recall-cache scope key is workspace-prefixed (`ws:<id>|<tier>:<id>`) so a byte-identical query under the same scope in two workspaces produces two distinct cache keys (no cross-workspace cache hit, EC-8). Skill `add`/`recall`/`consolidate` carry the workspace binding (a ws-A skill is invisible to a ws-B recall under the same `repoId`). The capacity + cron eviction passes scope their COUNT, candidate load, advisory lock, and nearest-neighbor isolation distance by workspace, so an eviction pass in ws-A can never soft-delete or be biased by a ws-B row. Proven by `tests/integration/workspace/cache-skill-eviction-isolation.test.ts` against real pgvector.
- Workspace RBAC management surface (multi-tenant-rbac-and-slice1-completion T2.1): a `MembersRepository` DIP (in-memory + pg impls) backs `POST/GET/PATCH/DELETE /v1/members`, gated by `requireRole('admin')` and scoped to the authenticated Principal's workspace (a cross-workspace member op is `not_found` → 404, no existence leak). The **last-owner invariant** is enforced ATOMICALLY in the pg impl (`db.transaction` + `SELECT ... FOR UPDATE` on the workspace's membership rows before the owner-count guard) so two concurrent owner-demotions can never leave a workspace with zero owners (the second serializes and returns `409 LAST_OWNER`). The admin key-mint (`POST /v1/admin/keys`) now accepts an optional `user_id` binding with an **anti-escalation guard**: minting a key bound to a member who outranks the caller is `403`, bound to a non-member is `422`, bound to a lower/equal member succeeds. Member request bodies use `.strict` zod schemas (a forged `workspaceId` field is rejected, never silently dropped).
- Workspace RBAC roles + role resolution (multi-tenant-rbac-and-slice1-completion T0.1/T1.1): `Principal` is widened to `{ workspaceId, userId, role, scopes }` and a new `WorkspaceRole` (`owner|admin|member`) + `roleSatisfies(role, min)` ordinal-rank helper + `DEFAULT_OWNER_USER_ID` are exported from `@usetheo/memory/contract`. `ApiKeyAuthVerifier.resolvePrincipal` now LEFT JOINs `workspace_users` on `(workspace_id, user_id)` to resolve the member's role; a key with no membership row resolves to the least-privileged `member` (default-deny). The env/no-credential bridge resolves to the seeded `default-owner` with role `owner`. A new `requireRole(min)` Hono middleware + `getPrincipal(c)` helper gate management routes (401 precedes 403). The bearer middleware now FAILS CLOSED: if the auth backend throws during role resolution it returns an explicit `503 SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE` and never falls through to a privileged default Principal.
- Workspace RBAC schema (multi-tenant-rbac-and-slice1-completion T0.2): migration `0017_workspace_members.sql` adds a `users` table (global identity) and a `workspace_users` M:N membership table (`(workspace_id, user_id)` composite PK + a `role` column constrained to `owner|admin|member` by a DB CHECK), plus a nullable `theo_api_keys.user_id` FK (`ON DELETE SET NULL`) binding a key to the member it acts as. Non-destructive + idempotent: seeds a stable `default-owner` user + its `default`-workspace owner membership (`ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING`) and backfills every legacy key's `user_id` to `default-owner`, so single-tenant deployments keep working. Role resolution LEFT JOINs `workspace_users` on `(workspace_id, user_id)`; a key with no membership resolves to the least-privileged `member` (default-deny).
- Workspace isolation foundation (multi-tenant-workspace-isolation T0.1): new `Principal { workspaceId, scopes }` + `Workspace { id, name, createdAt }` domain types and a `WorkspaceMismatch` error (maps to 404), exported from `@usetheo/memory/contract`. The `AuthVerifier` DIP gains an optional `resolvePrincipal(token): Promise<Principal | null>` alongside the existing boolean `verify` (ADR D2 additive-then-breaking; becomes required and replaces `verify` in a later phase).
- Workspace isolation schema (multi-tenant-workspace-isolation T0.2): migration `0016_workspaces.sql` adds a `workspaces` tenant table (seeded with a stable-id `default` workspace) and a denormalized `workspace_id text NOT NULL DEFAULT 'default'` column on all 6 directly-queried tables (`memories`/`entities`/`history`/`consolidation_history`/`reflection_counters`/`recall_cache`) plus a `workspace_id` FK on `theo_api_keys`. Non-destructive (existing rows backfill to `default` automatically); `(workspace_id, scope)` hot-path indexes added. The `memories` dedup unique index now leads with `workspace_id` so the same fact under the same user in two workspaces is two distinct rows.
- Workspace isolation persistence (multi-tenant-workspace-isolation T1.1): the `PgvectorStore` query path now filters by `workspace_id` (composed first, ahead of the tier filter) and the insert path writes it; a new `ScopedFilters` type makes `workspace_id` a compile-time-required field on the REST/server scoping contract. Cross-workspace recall is impossible at the store layer.
- Workspace isolation auth (multi-tenant-workspace-isolation T2.1): `ApiKeyAuthVerifier` + `BearerAuthVerifier` now implement `resolvePrincipal(token)` returning the credential's `{ workspaceId, scopes }` (a revoked/expired key resolves to `null` → 401); the bearer env key bridges to the `default` workspace. The REST `bearerAuth` middleware injects the resolved `Principal` into the request context and collapses the no-credential path to the `default`-workspace Principal instead of disabling auth.
- Workspace isolation end-to-end (multi-tenant-workspace-isolation T3.1): the `Memory` contract gains `withWorkspace(workspaceId)`, returning a memory view whose every scoped operation is bound to that workspace; the REST `remember`/`recall`/`reflect`/`forget`/`scopes` handlers now derive the workspace from the authenticated Principal (never the request body) and a cross-workspace `forget` by id returns 404 without touching the other workspace's row. THE GOAL TEST (`tests/integration/auth/workspace-isolation.test.ts`) proves the IDOR matrix against real pgvector: cross-workspace recall is empty, create derives the workspace from the binding, and cross-workspace forget is a 404. README Security section documents the model + the pre-release `AuthVerifier` breaking change.


### Changed

- PRIORITY 0 scope amendment (project-owner sign-off 2026-06-17): multi-tenant-flat workspace isolation — a `workspaces` tenant boundary resolved from the API-key credential, with `AuthVerifier` evolving from a boolean check to resolving a `Principal { workspaceId }` and every query scoped by the Principal's workspace — is re-classified as IN-SCOPE under P0 as CORE to the system (foundation of the v0.5 TheoCloud runtime). The three memory tiers (User/Session/Agent) remain as within-workspace partitions; `workspace_id` is the new outer anchor. Implementation tracked under slug `multi-tenant-workspace-isolation`. See CLAUDE.md § PRIORITY 0 Amendment 2026-06-17.


### Deprecated

- `AuthVerifier.verify(token): Promise<boolean>` is soft-deprecated in favor of `resolvePrincipal(token): Promise<Principal | null>` (workspace isolation). `verify` remains during the migration window; it is removed in a later phase.



### Fixed

- Integration test suite is now deterministically green so it can gate a production deploy — closed the last 5 flaky/stale failures surfaced after the v0.3.0 release (integration-suite-stabilization). The v0.4b long-tail eviction goal test no longer collapses on pgvector's approximate HNSW index for its synthetic worst-case fixture; two CLI boot-time tests no longer false-fail on absolute wall-clock budgets under suite load (now measured relative to a cold-start baseline); the CLI warm-up-cost changelog check follows the entry to its released section instead of pinning it to `[Unreleased]`; and the `npm pack` manifest hooks no longer time out under suite load. Two consecutive keyless runs report 0 failures (494 passed, 61 skipped). No product behavior changed — test-infrastructure hardening only.
